**Byte-identity is tested against `BuildInsert`'s real output, not a re-derivation, and the helper cannot pass vacuously.** `TestBuildMultiInsertUpsertMatchesSingleRow` builds both statements for the same target and compares from the clause marker onward; it `t.Fatalf`s when `BuildInsert` itself emits no clause, so a shape that stopped producing one fails rather than matching `""` on both sides. Three shapes cover every branch the dialects diverge on: a single key with update columns; a **composite key with nothing left to set** — the pure-link-table shape a batched junction upsert lands on, which is `DO NOTHING` on PostgreSQL/SQLite and the degenerate `` `k` = `k` `` self-assignment on MySQL; and `ResolvePKColumn` set, where FIX-196's `LAST_INSERT_ID()` must **lead** the MySQL set list and is ignored on the other two. A separate 10-case exact-SQL test pins the whole rendered statement per dialect, so the ordering facts are pinned too: the clause lands after the last value row and before `RETURNING`, the `len(ValueRows) == 0` early return still wins over a conflict target (an empty batch is `("", nil)`, not a bare `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT`), and a `sql.Default` sentinel leaves placeholder numbering intact since the clause appends no arguments of its own. **Next:** 27.5 (`UpsertMany`) — the first consumer, and the one sub-item in 27.1–27.7 where golden movement is expected across all 12 example modules, since `operations.upsert_many` defaults to `true`. 27.7 remains independent and is the shorter path to the 27.1–27.7 checkpoint.

**27.3 (single-row `Update` skip-refetch escape) complete 2026-09-17.** Ticket **C** / **D10** / **F1**: `Update` was the last entity-returning mutation that returned `c.Get(...)` unconditionally — `Create`, `CreateMany`, `Upsert`, `UpdateMany`, `UpdateWhere` and every delete variant have guarded their trailing read with `FieldOptions != nil && !HasSelectedColumns()` all along. One template, two guards, 61 regenerated goldens. `make check` clean and `make check-examples` green.

**The ticket's own rationale was wrong, and the auto-review caught it — the defect is worse than advertised.** **F1**, **D10** and 27.3's acceptance criterion all describe a wasted round-trip: "`Update` with an empty `FieldOptions` currently reads a row nobody asked for." It does not. `Get` delegates to `GetMany`, which **already** short-circuits on an all-false `FieldOptions` and returns `nil, nil`; `Get` then reads that empty result and returns **`ErrNotFound`**. So pre-27.3, `Update` under an empty `FieldOptions` issued **one** statement, not two — and returned `(nil, ErrNotFound)` after an `UPDATE` that had already committed. **That is a spurious-failure bug of the FIX-196 class, not a performance nit:** the caller sees not-found for a write that happened, rolls back its transaction, and on GraphQL maps to `NOT_FOUND`. The three lines fix it either way, which is how the wrong framing survived to implementation — but it changes the severity, and it explains why 27.9's `UpdateWithRelated` could not have composed `Update` with an empty `FieldOptions` at all. Re-running the pins against HEAD~1's generated code reports `sqlgen: resource not found`, not an extra statement. The criterion, `docs/design/archive/NESTED_MUTATIONS.md` **F1**/**D10**, and PRD §9.5/§9.7's unconditional "fetches and returns the current entity" prose are all corrected in place.

**Both sites are guarded, because §9.2 names both.** **F1** measured the terminal re-fetch after the `UPDATE`; the second site is the §9.5 *empty-update* short-circuit (`len(setClauses) == 0`), which 27.0 wrote into the PRD as "on the empty-update path as well as the normal one". Guarding only the terminal site would have left the documented no-op still reading a row nobody asked for — the same defect one branch over. With both in place, an empty-input `Update` under an empty `FieldOptions` touches the database **zero** times and returns `nil, nil`, where before it issued no statement and returned `ErrNotFound`. **The golden movement is confined to `Update` mechanically, not by assertion:** `git diff -U0 | grep '^@@' | grep -v ') Update('` is empty across all 62 changed files, the diff is insertions-only, and the added text collapses to exactly four distinct lines repeated 452 times — 226 `Update` methods × two sites.

**The placement is what keeps the skip free of side effects.** The guard sits after `m.AffectedPKs` / `m.AffectedTenants` are stamped on the `MutationContext`, so cache invalidation and event stamping see exactly the values they saw before: the escape drops a read, never a hook input. That spot is common to every arm of the template (`$captureTenant` × `supportsReturning` × `StrictUpdates`), so one guard covers all three dialects, tenanted and not, and `StrictUpdates`' `ErrNotFound` still fires ahead of it. **The reviewer's one open question was adjudicated by the user: `Get`'s `ErrNotFound` on an all-false `FieldOptions` is intentional, and is not a FIX.** The review flagged the asymmetry — `GetMany` returns `nil, nil` where `Get` returns `ErrNotFound` — as the root cause 27.3 patched one level above, and a tracked-FIX candidate. It is neither. The alternative spelling, letting a read short-circuit silently the way a mutation does, was considered and rejected: on a *read* an all-false selection is almost always a caller mistake, and a silent nil is indistinguishable from a legitimately absent row, so the mistake surfaces later and somewhere else. **A clear error shows the problem where it was made.** That reasoning was undocumented, which is why the review could only see the asymmetry — it is now recorded in **PRD §9.6**, alongside the corollary that makes 27.3 necessary: the read path is not where the mutation-side `nil, nil` contract gets spelled, so every entity-returning mutation must guard its own trailing read. A non-rendering `{{/* */}}` comment at the site in `get.go.tmpl` says the same thing to the next template maintainer, verified to move **zero** goldens. **27.9 inherits a documented contract rather than an open question.**

**Failing-first was verified by running it that way, and the first count of it was wrong** — against HEAD~1's generated code **two** of the four pins fail, not three, both with `sqlgen: resource not found`; the nil-FO and populated-FO pins pass at HEAD~1 by design, being inert-case regression pins. The earlier "three" came from reading a package-level `FAIL` instead of per-test output. A fifth test, the gen unit pin `TestUpdateTemplate_emptyFieldOptions`, was added in the review round to close a sibling-convention gap (`create_test.go` and `upsert_test.go` each pin their guard; `update_test.go` had none); it isolates the single-row `Update` body before counting, since `UpdateMany`/`UpdateWhere` carry their own guards and a whole-file count would pass with both of 27.3's sites missing. The four E2E pins: the query-count pin (one statement, it is the `UPDATE`, returns `nil`, and the write is confirmed landed on an *unwrapped* client so the verification is not counted against the escape), the zero-statement pin for the empty-update path, and the two inert cases — nil `FieldOptions` still yields `UPDATE` then `SELECT` and a *full* entity carrying columns the caller never selected, and a populated `FieldOptions` still re-fetches. 14.12's `TestUpdate_ZeroFields_NoUpdateSQL_Postgres` is untouched and still passes; it covers the empty-update path under a **nil** `FieldOptions`, which this change deliberately leaves alone. **Next:** 27.7 and the 27.4 → 27.5 → 27.6 chain, both independent — 27.7 is the shorter path to the 27.1–27.7 checkpoint, the 27.4 chain the longer one. **FIX-207 and FIX-208 are both Resolved**, so the two out-of-phase prerequisites no longer gate 27.6 or 27.9; 27.9's remaining dependency is 27.8 alone.

**27.1 (`FKOnTarget` hoist onto `RelationshipContext`) complete 2026-09-17.** Ticket **A** / **NW-D1**: the structural answer to "which table physically holds the FK column" is now derived once, in `fkColumnOnTarget`, and carried on `RelationshipContext` — where the write-side emitters can read it — instead of being computed inline inside `buildO2OJoinDetails` and thrown away. Three files, all under `cmd/sqlgen/gen/`. **`make check` clean and `make check-examples` green with the working tree unchanged afterwards — zero golden movement across all 12 example modules**, which is the whole acceptance criterion: this moves *where* a fact is computed, not what it is.

**The non-obvious part was the second entry point, not the derivation.** Neither `relationshipToContext` nor `configRelationshipToContext` took a `*parser.Schema`, so the hoist had to thread one through them and through `mergeDeclaredRelationships`. Deriving the field in `buildRelationshipContexts` — the obvious reading of "computed once in the relationship builder" — would have been **wrong**: `findO2ORelationships` (`context_table.go:2404`) builds the contexts for **chained** O2O joins by calling both constructors *directly*, bypassing that function entirely, so every chained join would have kept the zero value and flipped its `ON` clause the moment a chain crossed an FK-on-target edge. Deriving inside the two constructors covers both entry points by construction. **That is measured, not argued:** the wrong placement was implemented and run — `TestBuildO2OJoinDetails_chainedFKOnTarget` fails on it with `customers → addresses ON = customer_id/id, want id/customer_id`, and so does `TestE2EGoldenFiles`, because the examples already exercise a chained O2O. The unit pin is the faster and more legible of the two guards, not the only one. The retired column-scan pattern greps to nothing across `cmd/`, `parser/`, `sql/` and `database/`.

**`wireRelationshipFKMetadata`'s O2O arm was deliberately not folded in, and the reason is recorded on the field.** It probes the target's generated column set and falls back to the parent, which looks like the same question but is not: it resolves the FK column's *resolved nullability, Go type and Go field name*, so it must read the generated `ColumnContext` — post `column_map`, post `exclude_columns` — where `FKOnTarget` reads the parsed schema. Dispatching it on `FKOnTarget` would swap one lookup domain for another and could move `FKNullable` on any edge whose target table or FK column sits outside the generated set: golden movement for no payoff. **Per-type semantics are stated on the field** rather than left to inference — O2M `true` (FK on the many side), M2M `false` (both FKs on the junction, so neither *end* holds one), and a self-referential O2O `true`, which is what the read path has always produced and is harmless there because both sides of the `ON` clause are the same table's FK and PK either way. **`TestBuildTableContexts_relationshipFKOnTarget`** pins all four shapes through the real `parser.DetectRelationships` path, and closes on the acceptance criterion's second half: the two O2O edges agree on `Side == SideParent` while disagreeing on `FKOnTarget`, which is the single assertion proving `Side` cannot stand in for it. **The auto-review found no blocking item and four notes, all resolved in place:** two doc-accuracy corrections (the helper overstated that a schema-qualified config `table:` misses everywhere downstream — `markSyntheticFK` and 27.0a's discriminator validation both split it, so the layers genuinely disagree; and `false` is three answers in one, not just "FK on the source"), one test nit (the lookup closure `Fatalf`'d the parent `*testing.T` from inside subtests), and one forward risk with no action available here — **booked as two tasks on 27.8**, not as FIX entries: the write branch must not read `FKOnTarget == false` as "belongs-to", and `guidelines/TESTING.md` §11's fixture rule needs extending the moment a template reads the field. **Next:** 27.3, 27.7 and the 27.4 → 27.5 → 27.6 chain, all independent.

**27.2 (`discriminator:` read path) complete 2026-09-17.** The structured polymorphic edge now compiles on all three read paths, and the `assets` fixture's three invertible edges are migrated off `filter:` across all four example trees. **This is the sub-item where the PRD contradicted itself and the contradiction had to be settled rather than guessed.** §13.4.1 promised migration was "a byte-identical change to every generated read" while §4.8 and §13.4.1's own field tables promised the value is "bound as a parameter, never interpolated" — and the `filter:` path it must be byte-identical *to* interpolates. The O2O path made it sharper still: that predicate compiles into `sql.JoinClause.On`, whose contract (`sql/builder.go:80-87`) states builders "do not parse or number placeholders inside it" and that anything needing a parameter MUST move to the outer `Conditions` — **an invariant tenancy's placeholder ordering depends on**, and whose escape hatch does not apply here, because moving an O2O predicate from `ON` to `WHERE` converts the LEFT JOIN into an effective inner join and drops parents with no matching child. **Resolved by user decision: bind where a parameter channel exists, interpolate where it does not.** The loader and the relationship-filter `EXISTS` subquery bind the value and quote the identifier through the dialect — neither of which the raw-SQL `filter:` form can do; the O2O JOIN interpolates and stays byte-identical. PRD §13.4.1 now states the split as a table instead of an unqualified promise, and §4.8's row points at it.

**The dialect trap is the finding worth carrying forward.** The first implementation hand-assembled the O2O predicate as `alias.column = literal` — byte-identical on postgres and mysql, and **silently different on sqlite**, whose qualifier (rqlite/sql) emits `"alias"."column"` where pg_query and vitess emit bare identifiers. The fix makes equivalence structural rather than matched per dialect: the discriminator's equivalent filter text goes through the *same* `qualifyFilter` the `filter:` form does, so whatever that parser produces is what both forms produce. `TestDiscriminator_O2OJoinByteIdenticalToFilter` runs per dialect and fails on the hand-built form. Golden movement is confined to the two bound paths, verified by finding zero movement on every `On:` line across all four example trees; the manifest would otherwise have regressed (migrated edges lost their `filter` column outright), so `relationshipPredicate` reports a discriminator's equivalent predicate in that same field, restoring it byte-for-byte and sparing its frozen `schema_version` 0.1.0 a new key.

**D18(a)/(b)/(c) moved to 27.8.** All three are properties of the nested child input and the `connect` executor — types 27.7/27.8 introduce and that do not exist at 27.2's HEAD; §13.4.1 is explicit that the elision is "a property of the *nested* child input, which is a distinct type". D18(b) was already listed verbatim in 27.8's tasks, so it was carried in two places. 27.2 instead lands the data those rules consume: `RelationshipContext.Discriminator`, carrying the column and both literal renderings.

**Two pre-existing `filter:` bugs found and logged, neither folded in; neither reachable through `discriminator:`.** **FIX-213 (blocking)** — a `filter:` with a top-level `OR` is spliced without parentheses, and `AND` binds tighter than `OR`, so the parent correlation falls out and the loader returns *other parents' rows*; confirmed at runtime on SQLite (loading parent 1 returned a child owned by parent 2). It affects the o2m/m2m loader and the o2o JOIN `ON`; the `EXISTS` path already wraps, which is what marks the other two as an oversight. Present since 2026-05-13. Every `filter:` in the PRD, the fixtures and the examples is an `AND` — associative, needing no parens — which is exactly why nothing exercised it. **FIX-212 (tracked, since resolved)** — On SQLite a relationship filter over a `filter:` edge emitted SQL naming a literal alias `"sqlgenrel"` the subquery never defines: the runtime alias was spliced in by splitting on the *unquoted* token, and rqlite/sql's quoted output never matched. It hit the `EXISTS` path only, which is why everything compiled and passed. It predated this phase and affected the `filter:` form 27.2 does not touch. Resolved by deriving the alias spelling from each dialect's own qualifier, guarding the token in `filter:` input and asserting it cannot survive the splice; three SQLite golden sites moved, PostgreSQL and MySQL byte-unchanged. **Next:** 27.1, 27.3, 27.7 and the 27.4 → 27.5 → 27.6 chain, all independent.

**27.0a (config surface + JSON schema) complete 2026-09-17.** The whole nested-mutation config surface landed in one go, before any consumer of it: the four `Operations` toggles across all **five** `ExpandPreset` arms, `applyOperationOverrides` and `hasAnyOperationToggle`, the `apiOperationFields` / `clientOnlyOperationFields` split (the three `…_with_related` are maskable, `upsert_many` is client-only), both `nested_mutations` blocks, `RelationshipDiscriminator`, the `relationshipDedupKey` discriminator component, five §4.13 validation rules and the `schema/v1.json` entries for all of it. Eight files, all under `cmd/sqlgen/config/`. **`make check` clean and `make check-examples` green with the working tree unchanged afterwards — zero golden movement across all 12 example modules**, exactly as the structural scan predicted: `gen.ResolvedOperations` and `maskAPIOperations` are both hand-written field lists, which is the firewall that keeps generated output still. Every rule was verified failing-first by reverting it, including the schema drift test itself — deleting two keys from `v1.json` reports six missing paths across all five `Operations` `$ref` sites.

**The auto-review found six items; all six are resolved in place, none logged as a FIX.** Two were code: `discriminator.column`, `discriminator.value` and `nested_mutations.relationships[].name` are marked **Required** in PRD §4.8 / §13.4.1 and were enforced by `schema/v1.json` but **not** by Go — the loader was laxer than the schema it ships, and a missing `value` had teeth beyond the asymmetry, rendering `<nil>:<nil>` in the dedup key so two value-less edges collide as duplicates over a value neither declares; `validateNestedRequiredKeys` closes it. And `TestSchemaV1_acceptsUnexercisedSurface` gained six positive cases, since the drift test checks *presence*, not shape, and no example config uses a new key — nothing validated an actual document against the three new `$defs`. One was a forward risk booked as a **task on 27.8** rather than a FIX: `applyOperationOverrides` does not propagate a base override onto its nested toggle, so `operations: {preset: all, create: false}` resolves to `Create=false, CreateWithRelated=true` — validation deliberately stays quiet there, which makes `toResolvedOperations` the only remaining guard. The last three were docs: `docs/configuration.md` was missing all four toggles and both new blocks, 27.7's *Tests Required* still listed the two items its own Tasks list had struck (including the "six presets" count 27.0a corrected to five), and 27.0a's own acceptance line overclaimed — `discriminator:` edges are **accepted** here but generate an unfiltered loader until 27.2 adds the read path, a bounded interval now stated in the record rather than left implied.

**Two judgment calls are recorded in the phase file rather than left implicit.** **D20** fires on an *explicitly set* `…_with_related: true` whose base resolves false, not on the resolved pair: the resolved reading would reject `operations: {create: false}` under the default `all` preset, punishing every config that disables a base operation while never mentioning nested mutations, and §4.13's own rationale — *"the user set a flag and got nothing with no signal"* — is the explicit reading. `api.operations` is exempt, since a mask is subtractive over the client's surface (§26.5.1) *(superseded by FIX-229 once 27.10 made the mask conjoin the base)*. Separately, `RelationshipDiscriminator.Value` is typed **`any`, not `string`**: the PRD types it `scalar` in both §4.8 and §13.4.1, deliberately contrasting with `column`'s `string`, so the dedup key renders it `%T:%v` to keep `value: 1` and `value: "1"` distinct declarations. **Next:** 27.1, 27.2, 27.3, 27.7 and the 27.4 → 27.5 → 27.6 chain can now run in parallel.

**27.0 (PRD sync — P1–P29) complete 2026-09-16.** `docs/PRD.md` only, +502/−20, no code and no golden movement. 28 of the 29 deltas landed and **P17 is struck** — §9.5/§9.7 already carry it from FIX-196, re-read at HEAD before striking. The centrepiece is a **new §9.9 Nested Mutations** in ten subsections, written to stand alone: the four write shapes, the FK-nullability axis that decides the verb set, the eligibility matrix, **E1–E12**, the five generated type families, the execution order, the query count, error attribution, observability and tenancy/access. Around it: §9.2 gains `UpsertMany` and the three `…WithRelated` rows with the **C3** non-folding note; §13.1 splits belongs-to from has-one and makes `FKOnTarget` and FK nullability first-class; **new §13.4.1** specifies `discriminator:` and **D18**'s three write-side rules; §4.6/§4.8 carry the `nested_mutations` config and the four new operations toggles with a paragraph pinning every `ExpandPreset` arm; §4.13 gains **six** validation rows (the three named plus **D20**, the closed-set rule and the discriminator-column rule); §22.1/§22.2/§22.3 carry the two sentinels, the `NestedMutationError` struct and the per-edge-per-verb wrapping pattern; `OpUpsertMany` joins the §21.2 constant list with **D16**; §25.1 gains a **write-side** subsection stating `ceil(N / batch_size)` per table per verb with **Q9**'s three measured bind ceilings; §26.5.1/§26.5.5/§26.12 carry the GraphQL projection, the two error codes and the v1 limitations; §27.7/§28.9 carry the observability contract (**P29**); §29.4.2a and §29.10 carry the `connect` tenant check; §32.5 carries **E6**/**E7**. **Q10** (declared order = alphabetical by relationship name) is normative in §9.9.5 with the three places it is observable. `make check` clean; the path-based E2E and security triggers correctly did not fire on a docs-only diff. Two things fixed in place beyond the P-list: §22.1 was missing `ErrRefreshConcurrentlyInTx` (shipped since Phase 21), and four pre-existing dangling intra-PRD anchors — the document now has **zero**. **One thing deliberately left open**, per the phase file's own split: §9.5's three `UpsertMany` decisions (the `DO NOTHING` return contract, the MySQL db-generated-PK contract, `AffectedPKs` from inputs) belong to **27.5**; 27.0 landed only the dedupe rule the design had already settled by measurement, so 27.5 cannot pick first-wins by accident. **The auto-review round found seven items and all seven are fixed in place**, one of which was a *missing* delta with teeth: §13.7.1's relationship dedup key is `(target, fk, filter)`, so 27.2's own fixture migration would have collapsed three `discriminator:` edges into `documents`/`entity_id` onto one key and hard-errored as duplicates on the first run — the key now carries a discriminator component. Three more were pre-existing PRD staleness the new text would otherwise have cited (§9.5's "the PK is always a valid conflict target", which `pkConflictTargetIsIndexed` has gated since `336f591`; §22.1's blanket re-export claim, false for `ErrRefreshConcurrentlyInTx`; and `upsert_many`'s undocumented API projection, adjudicated **client-only** since no batch-upsert mutation exists). **That review also surfaced a regression risk that produced a new sub-item.** `cmd/sqlgen/config/schema/v1.json` is `additionalProperties: false`, and `TestSchemaV1_noDriftFromConfigStructs` (`cmd/sqlgen/config/schema_test.go:309-395`) is reflection-driven and **path-aware**, so it fails on any Go config field with no schema property at its path — yet the file was named by no sub-item, and the config keys were split across 27.2, 27.5 and 27.7. Worse, the two failure modes disagree: `generation.nested_mutations`, `tables.<t>.nested_mutations` and `relationships[].discriminator` hard-error under `KnownFields(true)`, while `operations.*` keys are **silently accepted and dropped**, because `Operations` defines its own `UnmarshalYAML` and `yaml.Node.Decode` builds a fresh non-strict decoder the setting cannot reach (`config.go:1441-1444`). **New sub-item 27.0a lands the entire config surface in one go** — the four `Operations` toggles across all five `ExpandPreset` arms (five, not six: `all`/`""` is one arm plus an erroring `default`), `applyOperationOverrides`, the `apiOperationFields` / `clientOnlyOperationFields` split, both `nested_mutations` blocks, `RelationshipDiscriminator`, the `relationshipDedupKey` component 27.2's fixture migration needs, the five config-answerable §4.13 validation rules, and the four `schema/v1.json` `$defs` edits. A structural scan confirmed it moves **zero goldens**: all eleven consumers of `config.Operations` are hand-written field lists, the manifest emits no `operations` key and its `Relationship` struct has no `discriminator` slot, MCP reads none of it, and `gen.ResolvedOperations` — the generator's separate 17-bool view — is deliberately not extended there. **Next:** **27.0a**, then 27.1, 27.2, 27.3, 27.7 and the 27.4 → 27.5 → 27.6 chain in parallel.

**Phase 27 (Nested Mutations) broken down 2026-09-16 — the first phase with open work since Phase 16 closed.** 12 sub-items in `docs/tracker/phase-27.md`, mapped from `docs/design/archive/NESTED_MUTATIONS.md` §9's ten tickets plus a PRD sync and a closure sweep. **27.0 leads and blocks everything** — none of the 29 PRD deltas (**P1–P29**) is written, so per project rule 1 no code lands first. The design doc was re-verified against HEAD the same day: all ten structural gaps still hold and no decision was invalidated, but three things had moved since its `dec00eb` evidence baseline — Phase 26 bound nullable UUIDs to `*uuid.UUID` (**F11**, a new silent-no-op hazard on `disconnect`/`clear`, since `omittable.Set[*T](nil)` and `omittable.Value[*T]{}` are now interchangeable to the compiler), `3509180` added an eighteenth fixture edge, and `336f591` gated conflict-target emission on schema evidence of an index (a new precondition for every nested upsert). 24 of 55 load-bearing citations had drifted and were repaired.

**Two questions settled during the breakdown, one by measurement.** **Q9** (do `connect`/`disconnect` batch, or inherit a parameter ceiling?) assumed PostgreSQL's 65535 was the bound. Probed against `postgres:16-alpine`, `mysql:8.0` and `modernc.org/sqlite`: **SQLite stops at 32766**, half the other two, and no dialect's error names a table, column, edge or verb — PostgreSQL's is a bare `errors.errorString` raised client-side by pgx, so `errors.As` has nothing structural to match. Resolved to batching at `batchSize` (default 200), which clears the worst case by two orders of magnitude and makes **D12**'s contract uniform at `ceil(N / batchSize)`; `clear` stays at exactly 1 because it names no ids. **Q10** (what "declared order" means) resolved to alphabetical by relationship name — total, independent of the optional config block, and already the generator's tie-break in `context_cache.go`.

**Two FIXes logged as phase prerequisites, both true at HEAD independently of the feature.** **FIX-207** — the §32.3 event-redaction switch's `default` arm publishes the raw input; filed as a latent class and found **reachable on shipped code**, since `SoftDeleteWhere` / `HardDeleteWhere` / `RestoreWhere` set `Input` to the caller's filter and `UserFilter` carries `password_hash` (`access: internal`) and `new_password` (`access: write_only`) as filterable comparators. Blocks 27.6. **FIX-208** — the non-tenanted `OpUpdateWhere` cache arm wipes the table's whole namespace while the tenanted arm beside it evicts precisely; O2M `connect`/`disconnect` both compile to `UpdateWhere`. Blocks 27.9, and filed with three candidate resolutions rather than a foregone conclusion.

**Checkpoint built into the order:** 27.1–27.7 each ship value standalone — `UpsertMany` is a real gap in the write surface, the single-row `Update` refetch is a wasted round-trip today, and `discriminator:` is the first structured description of a polymorphic edge in the config — so the end of 27.7 is where the emitters (27.8–27.10) can be decided on fresh information rather than up front.

**`UUID_MIGRATION.md` and `GRAPHQL_READ_SURFACE.md` archived 2026-09-16.** Both moved to `docs/design/archive/` alongside `GRAPHQL.md`, on the same test that qualified it: a `SYNCED` design doc for a closed phase (26 and 25 respectively) with **zero Go citations and zero PRD back-references**, so nothing in the code or the spec depends on it. Their existing status blocks were already precise about what each is retained for — the UUID measurement record (§11, findings measured against `go1.27.1` / `pgx v5.9.1` / `modernc.org/sqlite v1.48.1` / PostgreSQL 16), the driver and wire-format analysis (§3, §6) and rollback reasoning (§12); and the read-surface decision record (D1–D11), gap analysis (F1–F9) and rejected/deferred record (§7) — so those notes were preserved verbatim under a new **ARCHIVED** header that adds the "do not read this for current behavior" line and a link to the superseding PRD section. 33 inbound references repointed across `IMPLEMENTATION_ORDER.md`, `STATUS.md`, `fixes.md`, `phase-25.md`, `phase-26.md`, `NESTED_MUTATIONS.md` and `TENANCY_IMPROVEMENTS.md`.

**Why these two and nothing else.** The full `docs/` sweep that preceded this found no further candidates. `CACHE.md` (463 Go citations), `MCP.md` (124, four inside user-visible error strings) and `MANIFEST.md` (three live PRD delegations) are load-bearing supplements the PRD points *at*. `configuration.md` and `cli-manifest.md` are consumer docs linked from the README. `TENANCY.md` is Phase 13's sibling. And `NESTED_MUTATIONS.md`, `NESTED_MUTATIONS_EXAMPLES.md`, `TENANCY_IMPROVEMENTS.md`, `MUTATION.md` and `SCHEMAFUZZ.md` describe work that was never built — archiving those would bury backlog rather than retire finished work, which is the opposite of what `docs/design/archive/` is for. Two of them (`MUTATION.md`, `SCHEMAFUZZ.md`) have been `DESIGN FROZEN` since 2026-05-15 with no phase ever scheduled, which is a "do we still want these?" question rather than an archival one.

**13.11 (tenancy lint rule) cancelled 2026-09-16 — Phase 13 closes, and no phase is open.** The sub-item proposed extending `sqlgen lint` with a warning-level rule flagging `Raw` / `RawExec` call sites on tenanted tables, gated on Phase 12.12's `golang.org/x/tools/go/packages` infrastructure. **It contradicted the PRD as it stands.** §29.11 "Constraints" lists exactly this as an explicit non-goal — *"There is no tenancy-specific lint rule. `ErrMissing` at the first tenanted call is a strictly better signal than speculative static analysis: it fires on the real code path"* — and §23.7's `sqlgen lint` rule set carries three rules, none of them tenancy-related. 13.11's own **PRD Reference** line cited §29.11, the section that says the rule should not exist. Cancelling is therefore the tracker catching up to the spec, not a scope reduction against it, and **no PRD amendment is required**. The sub-item's task list is retained under a Cancelled status so the rejected design stays legible rather than vanishing.

Three consequences. **Phase 13 closes at 10/10 with one cancelled sub-item**, taking the shape Phase 12 already uses (`Complete (12.12 deferred)`) — with the row corrected to `10 + 1 cancelled` so the count and the status agree, which is the mismatch the Phase 16 closure flagged one entry below. **12.12 is unaffected and stays deferred on its own merits**, but the "pairs naturally with the §29 tenant-aware lint rule" half of its deferral rationale is now void — it is the sole claimant on that lint infrastructure, so its cost/benefit is worth re-reading before anyone schedules it; Deferred Items records this. **The runtime guarantee is unchanged** — `SkipTenancy` remains the explicit, greppable bypass (§29.4.3) and `ErrMissing` still fires at the first tenanted call in a handler that forgot to resolve a tenant, which is the behavior §29.11 argues is superior to the lint rule in the first place.

**Next:** phases 0–26 are all Complete; **Phase 27 is broken down and Not Started** (see the top of this section — this line read "every phase 0–26 is now Complete" until 27 was opened on 2026-09-16). The immediate next action is **27.0**, the PRD sync, which gates all code in the phase. Remaining open work outside it is the standing deferred set — 12.12 (above), the manifest JSON Schema v1.0.0 freeze carried since Phase 18, and four open FIXes (**FIX-207** phase 11, **FIX-208** phase 12, **FIX-209** phase 23 and **FIX-210** phase 18 — the first two logged as Phase 27 prerequisites; **FIX-210** is the only `blocking` one, filed 2026-09-16 for a `RemoveAll` of `<output.dir>/manifest/` that carries no ownership check). **FIX-199** and **FIX-200** are both resolved: FIX-200 closed 2026-09-16 as documented behavior — its diagnosis held, but the proposed default flip would have needed a second §27.2 exception, so §27.7 gained a junction-cache subsection and the per-table opt-out stands as the answer. Two items surfaced during the 2026-09-16 docs sweep are still unlogged and worth a decision: `docs/design/TENANT_SCOPED_REFERENCES.md` documents an unclosed cross-tenant FK-write gap that the PRD cites but no FIX or phase tracks, and the Phase 25 release-footer gap (three consumer-visible behavior changes with no `!` or `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer) remains amendable only until the first release consumes those commits.

**Phase 16 (GraphQL API Generation) closed 2026-09-16 — the last phase with active work outstanding.** All 9 sub-items complete: **16.1** schema generation + scalar marshaling (`MarshalGQL`/`UnmarshalGQL` on `types.JSONMap` / `DateTime` / `NullDateTime`, stdlib-only); **16.2** the `sqlgen graphql {init,gen}` wrapper subcommand, merging sqlgen's required `models:` bindings into a temp config so the consumer's `gqlgen.yml` stays byte-equal on disk; **16.3** curated resolver scaffolding; **16.4** the recursive field-selection walker; **16.5** filter / sort / pagination translators; **16.6** sentinel → `extensions.code` error mapping + middleware; **16.7** multi-schema namespacing + per-table API gating; **16.8** the E2E example module, decomposed into 16.8a–g; **16.9** this closure.

**16.9 closure sweep (landed 2026-09-16):** `make check` clean across all 8 modules under `-race` (1m29.8s; longest leg `cmd/sqlgen/gen` ~47.5s); `make check-examples` clean across all 12 example modules (2m00.2s; longest leg `examples/graphql/tests` ~43.4s); `make test-integration` clean across all 8 modules under `-race` (1m31.1s; longest legs `cmd/sqlgen` ~52.0s, `cmd/sqlgen/gen` ~51.5s, `cmd/sqlgen/cli` ~33.4s); no flakes. **FIX triage:** 0 entries originate in Phase 16 — the ~20 the 16.8 bring-up surfaced (FIX-064, FIX-065–FIX-084) were logged and resolved during the phase; 2 carried forward, both `tracked` and both pre-dating it (**FIX-199**, phase 13 — the tenant column reaches `<T>IncrementColumn` on the model side, needing the tenancy-side fix at `isIncrementEligible`, untouched by the GraphQL work; **FIX-200**, phase 12 — junction tables are cached but no generated read path reads them through the cache, a caching-layer issue). **Surface delta summary (across Phase 16):** sqlgen grew a generated GraphQL layer in front of the unified client. Twelve new templates under `cmd/sqlgen/gen/templates/api/` emit the per-table `*_gen.graphqls` schema, the seeded resolvers, the `sqlgenresolver/` helper package, and the scalar / filter / sort / input translators; a new `cmd/sqlgen/wrapper/` package implements the Shape A flow (seed-on-absent, panic-stub rewriter, `mergeResolverScaffold`, `mergeSingleFileResolver`) behind the `sqlgen graphql {init,gen}` subcommand, invoking gqlgen as a subprocess so `cmd/sqlgen` never imports it and the runtime module stays stdlib-only. A new postgres-only testcontainer-backed example (`testdata/examples/graphql/`, 9 tables including a composite-PK tenanted fixture) pins the surface end-to-end across 13 runtime test files. **PRD reconciliation:** no recorded §26 divergence at closure — but the substantive work was done by later phases, not here: 25.0 and 26.0 were both dedicated PRD-sync sub-items that amended §26 after Phase 16's original 2026-04-29 sync. The closure confirms no *recorded* divergence remains; it did not re-audit §26 line-by-line against the generator. **Next:** no phase has active work. Phase 13's Overview row still reads `In Progress`, but only because 13.11 (lint rule) is a stretch item gated on Phase 12.12 infrastructure and explicitly deferred — the same deferral Phase 12's row already records as `Complete (12.12 deferred)`; the two rows state one situation in two different shapes and are worth reconciling next time either is touched. Remaining open work is the standing deferred set — 13.11, 12.12, the manifest JSON Schema v1.0.0 freeze carried since Phase 18, and the two carried-forward FIXes above. Also still open from Phase 25's closure: **no Phase 25 commit carries a `!` or `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer** despite three consumer-visible behavior changes, which Release Please would render as ordinary features.

**`docs/GRAPHQL.md` archived 2026-09-16 — one 16.9 task, not the sub-item.** Moved to `docs/design/archive/GRAPHQL.md` (new directory) with a redirect header naming PRD §26 as normative and recording what the file is still retained for: the §19 open questions, the §17 testing-strategy notes, and the §6 cross-library convention comparisons that motivated the curated resolver surface. The header also notes that §26 has moved since the 2026-04-29 sync — Phases 25 and 26 both amended it — so the archived text is a design record, not a current reference. All 44 inbound references across `IMPLEMENTATION_ORDER.md`, `phase-16.md`, `fixes.md`, `phase-25.md`, `MANIFEST.md`, `MCP.md`, `NESTED_MUTATIONS.md` and `.claude/commands/close-phase.md` were repointed; the 16.9 archival task and its acceptance criterion are ticked. **16.9 itself stays Not Started** — its three remaining tasks (the `make check` / `check-examples` / `test-integration` sweeps, `/fix` triage, and the PRD §26-vs-landed-codegen reconciliation) are untouched, so Phase 16 remains the one genuinely open phase.

**Docs-consolidation read (2026-09-16, no action taken beyond the above).** Asked whether the five SYNCED design docs could be folded into the PRD and deleted. They do not behave alike. `docs/design/archive/UUID_MIGRATION.md` and `docs/design/archive/GRAPHQL_READ_SURFACE.md` carry zero code citations and zero PRD back-references, so they are removable — but what they are retained for (measurement methodology against pinned driver versions; the D1–D11 decision record and F1–F9 gap analysis) is not spec material, so *archiving* rather than merging is the fit. `MANIFEST.md` has three live PRD delegations (§5.3 `sql_bodies` shape, §5.1.1 conventions rationale) that must be absorbed first. `MCP.md` is the outlier: **124 Go citations, four of them inside user-visible error strings** (`config/validate.go:1876-1909`, `"… (MCP.md §3.4)"`), and PRD §31.2 delegates the agent-integration walkthrough to MCP.md §3.2 outright. PRD §31.2 does cover §3.4's validation rules, so those four strings could retarget to `PRD §31.2` — but the walkthrough needs a home before the file can go. Deferred by user direction; revisit if the duplication starts costing something.

**Phase 25 (GraphQL Read Surface Completion) closed 2026-09-16.** All 13 sub-items complete, across four tickets: **25.0** the PRD sync that blessed deltas B1–B8 (`docs/PRD.md` +205/−15, written first per project rule 1 and cited by every later sub-item; B8 landed the same day once the D11 correlation-qualifier decision settled); **Ticket A — 25.1** one field map collapsing the filter+sort projection to a single derivation (the load-bearing structural fix: schema and translator each derived the same fact from different inputs, so a disagreement was accepted-and-ignored — silent wrong results); **25.3** the five shipped comparator families plus `DecimalComparator`; **25.4** enum comparators monomorphized per enum; **25.5** JSON/JSONB/Slice comparators monomorphized; **25.2** the completeness lint that makes schema/translator drift unlandable (`gen/api_lint.go`, wired into `buildOneAPITable`/`buildOneAPIView` so it hard-errors under both `sqlgen generate` and `sqlgen validate`); **Ticket B — 25.6** view tenancy detection + attachment (zero golden churn by design — no example paired `tenancy.enabled: true` with a view until 25.7 landed the fixture) and **25.7** the read-path emission that actually closes the cross-tenant view read hole; **Ticket C — 25.8** views into `APIContext` (context, config, gating, with D9 satisfied by sequencing 25.7 first so the Go-client tenant predicate was in place before any view reached HTTP) and **25.9** view GraphQL emission + gqlgen binding, proven by the F1+F3 two-tenant E2E over the real gqlgen server; **Ticket D — 25.10** the runtime prefix-exempt correlated `sql.Exists` condition (D11; four files in `sql/`, no golden churn because nothing emitted it yet), **25.11** its payoff — model-side relationship filter fields compiled to correlated `EXISTS` carrying the target's own soft-delete and tenant predicates inside the subquery — and **25.12** the GraphQL projection of the same.

**Closure sweep (landed 2026-09-16):** `make check` clean across all 8 modules under `-race` (1m18s; longest leg `cmd/sqlgen/gen` ~48.6s); `make check-examples` clean across all 12 example modules (1m57s; longest leg `examples/graphql/tests` ~42.8s); `make test-integration` clean across all 8 modules under `-race` (1m31s; longest legs `cmd/sqlgen` ~52.3s, `cmd/sqlgen/gen` ~51.9s, `cmd/sqlgen/cli` ~22.2s); no flakes in any leg. **FIX triage:** the phase logged and resolved a large inline backlog during its run — 25.0 alone logged 7 and resolved all 7, 25.3 logged 11 across two verify rounds (10 resolved, FIX-145 closed later as Ticket A's family work completed), and FIX-177/178/179 (the two spec-level PRD §11.2 contradictions 25.5 made reachable, plus its auto-review's `PrefixConditions` find) were resolved 2026-09-02; 25.9's auto-review surfaced two **pre-existing** defects that were logged rather than folded in (FIX-184, FIX-185) and have since been resolved. **0 entries remain open against Phase 25**; 2 carried forward, both `tracked` and both pre-dating the phase (**FIX-199**, phase 13 — the tenant column reaches `<T>IncrementColumn` on the model side, a tenancy-side fix at `isIncrementEligible`, untouched by this phase's read-surface work. **FIX-200**, phase 12 — junction tables are cached by default but no generated read path reads them through the cache, gated on hoisting `IsJunction` onto `parser.Table`).

**Surface delta summary (across Phase 25).** The GraphQL read surface stopped lying in three distinct ways. Filter and sort fields are now projected from **one** derivation instead of two independent ones, a completeness lint makes a future divergence fail generation rather than silently mistranslate, and the `StringComparator` catch-all is **deleted** — the two shapes with no sound GraphQL face (a non-schema-enum PascalCase Go type; an array element outside the five measured built-ins) are now absent from both surfaces rather than advertised and ignored, which is the only outcome §26.5.3 permits. Filtering itself got materially wider: `DecimalComparator` plus the five shipped families completed, enum comparators monomorphized per enum, and JSON/JSONB/Slice comparators projected — with the dialect truths measured rather than assumed (postgres `json` document operators cast to `jsonb`; the JSON family is gated out entirely on SQLite). **Views became first-class on the read surface, and that is the phase's security fix**: a tenanted view read was previously unscoped, and now filters — views also start appearing in the generated GraphQL schema, opt out with `views.<n>.api.enabled: false`. Finally, every list relationship now contributes a `*<Target>Filter` member to its parent's `<T>Filter`, compiled to a correlated `EXISTS` that carries the target's own soft-delete and tenant predicates **inside** the subquery and is gated on the target's own API exposure so a relationship into an `api.enabled: false` entity is not a read side-channel; the tenant reaches `ToConditions` through a new variadic `FilterOption` (a user-blessed PRD §11.1 amendment, taken because §11.1 requires a bound tenant parameter in the subquery while pinning `ToConditions` to a signature with no ctx and no client), which also carries the nesting depth so a self-referential filter nested inside itself cannot shadow its own correlation. **Release notes — a gap to close before the first release:** the phase's own header lists two consumer-visible behavior changes that "must be called out at phase closure" (a tenanted view read starts filtering; views start appearing in the schema), and 25.2's removal of the `StringComparator` fallback removes previously-advertised filter fields — but **no Phase 25 commit carries a `!` or a `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer**, so Release Please would render all three as ordinary features. The repository has no configured remote and no release has consumed these footers yet, so this is still closable by amending; flagged rather than fixed at closure time, since amending is a history rewrite. **Next:** **Phase 16 is the one phase still genuinely open** — 16.1–16.8g are Complete (16.8 umbrella closed 2026-05-14) but **16.9**, its own closure-bookkeeping sub-item (PRD §26 sync, `docs/design/archive/GRAPHQL.md` archival, tracker update), is still **Not Started**; note that `docs/design/archive/GRAPHQL.md` was independently flipped to SYNCED on 2026-04-29, so part of 16.9's stated work may already be done and the sub-item needs a read before it is scheduled. Otherwise: Phase 13's 13.11 (lint rule, gated on 12.12) and Phase 12's 12.12 remain explicitly deferred stretch items, and the manifest JSON Schema v1.0.0 freeze is still a deferred parallel track carried since Phase 18.

**Phase 26 (Standard Library UUID as First-Class) closed 2026-09-15.** All 7 sub-items complete: **26.0** PRD sync making the stdlib binding normative (§7.2/§7.3/§7.4/§26.4.1/§4.13 rewritten, §28.8's hardcoded `google/uuid` event-ID mandate replaced, the false "UUID v4/v7 generation using the selected library" bullet dropped rather than restated); **26.1** the `uuidstd` integration package (registered first in `knownIntegrations`; every acceptance criterion already satisfied by existing resolver code, verified by reading before writing); **26.2** the one-UUID-library-per-generated-package rule (`gen/uuid_library.go`, written against resolved contexts rather than config text, so all three claim surfaces — global `overrides.types`, table-scoped, `column_map.<col>.import` — are covered by one predicate); **26.3** the `detectIntegrations` global-vs-table asymmetry fix, plus the removal of the non-existent `views.<view>.overrides.types` from the PRD and config by user decision; **26.3a** per-integration UUID generation expressions (`gotype.UUIDIntegration` carrying `V4Expr`/`V7Expr`/`V4StringExpr`/`V7StringExpr` off the integration packages, keyed by import path, wired into `create`/`upsert` and the event-hooks file — the spellings are stored rather than derived because they are not derivable); **26.4** the default flip (four lines in `gotype.go`; PostgreSQL only, MySQL/SQLite confirmed untouched by reading their mapping literals); **26.5** the example migration (five to stdlib, `postgres_stdlib` held on google and `graphql_null_wrappers` moved to gofrs v5.5.1 as the two wrapper guards).

**26.4 + 26.5 shipped together as the breaking pair; closure sweep (landed 2026-09-15):** `make check` clean across all 8 modules under `-race` (1m14s; longest leg `cmd/sqlgen/gen` ~45.8s); `make check-examples` clean across all 12 example modules (1m59s; longest leg `examples/graphql/tests` ~43.9s); `make test-integration` clean across all 8 modules under `-race` (1m32s; longest legs `cmd/sqlgen` ~51.7s, `cmd/sqlgen/gen` ~51.3s, `cmd/sqlgen/cli` ~20.9s), E2E green on PostgreSQL, MySQL and SQLite; no flakes in any leg. **FIX triage:** 0 entries logged against Phase 26 — every defect the phase surfaced was fixed inline per the standing in-place-fix preference; 2 carried forward, both `tracked` and both pre-dating the phase (**FIX-199**, phase 13 — the tenant column reaches `<T>IncrementColumn` on the model side; untouched by the UUID work and needs the tenancy-side fix at `isIncrementEligible`. **FIX-200**, phase 12 — junction tables are cached by default but no generated read path reads them through the cache; a cache-default change gated on hoisting `IsJunction` onto `parser.Table`, orthogonal to UUID binding).

**Surface delta summary (across Phase 26).** A PostgreSQL `uuid` column with no `overrides.types.uuid` now resolves to the standard library's `uuid.UUID` (`uuid[]` → `[]uuid.UUID`) instead of Go `string`, which moves generated struct fields, method signatures, manifest `go_type` values, the GraphQL scalar a `uuid` primary key binds (`ID` → `UUID`), and `TenantResolver[string]` → `TenantResolver[uuid.UUID]`; the standard library ships no null wrapper, so a nullable UUID is `*uuid.UUID` guarded with `!= nil` / `*v` rather than `uuid.NullUUID` with `.Valid` / `.UUID`, and the `NullUUID` GraphQL scalar with its `MarshalNullUUID` / `UnmarshalNullUUID` pair is gone from stdlib-bound packages (filters stay on `NullableIDComparator` — the comparator keys on the SQL type, not the integration). `github.com/google/uuid` and `github.com/gofrs/uuid/v5` remain first-class opt-outs via `overrides.types.uuid`, and the generator no longer assumes one library's spelling anywhere: 26.3a made v4/v7 generation per-integration and 26.5's gofrs migration caught `scalars.go.tmpl`'s hardcoded `uuid.Parse` — which gofrs spells `FromString` — closing both through the same registry seam. Consumers gain a **Go 1.27 module floor** when the standard library binding is in play (`go vet` rejects the import below it even though `go build` accepts it) and lose the forced `github.com/google/uuid` dependency for events-enabled packages: event IDs now follow the selected integration, so a stdlib-bound or UUID-free package needs no UUID module at all. Two properties were narrowed by measurement rather than assumed — a non-`uuid` column retyped to stdlib `uuid.UUID` does **not** scan under pgx (`UUIDCodec` keys on the OID; the FIX-156 fixture moved to gofrs-bound `graphql_null_wrappers` and PRD §7.4 now scopes the claim), and `database/sql`'s special case was probed against a real container on lib/pq before the "no `Valuer`/`Scanner` needed" claim was allowed to stand. **Two coverage observations carried forward, neither logged as a FIX:** no golden pins `use_pointers: false` × the default stdlib binding (only `TestDefaultUUIDBinding` does), and `scalarModelPath`'s silent drop of an external scalar's `models:` entry now sits on the path of every PostgreSQL project with a `uuid` column rather than only opted-in ones. **Release notes:** the `eb58ae2` `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer carries the retype, the opt-outs and the Go 1.27 floor; the `NullUUID`/`.Valid` change and the dropped event dependency are recorded in `phase-26.md` and here only — closable by amending that footer before the first release, since the repository has no configured remote and Release Please has consumed nothing yet. **Next:** **`/close-phase 25`** — Phase 25 (GraphQL Read Surface Completion) has had all 13 sub-items Complete since 2026-09-03 and is the one closure still outstanding; Phase 26 closed ahead of it.

**Phase 24 (Event Bus — First-Class NATS Transport) closed 2026-07-31.** All 11 sub-items complete: **24.1** `json` snake_case tags on `event.Event` (wire hygiene, `metadata,omitempty`; zero golden churn); **24.2** additive `event.Config.MetadataFunc` + the single generator hook-entry merge (`mergeEventMetadata`, per-event clone, system `"tenant"` stamp applied last/unoverridable, nil→byte-identical, D6); **24.3** natsbus `WithSubscribeErrorHandler`/`WithRetry` (core-mode observability, D2/D3); **24.4** natsbus header plumbing + `Sqlgen-Envelope-Version` (out-of-band wire headers, EB-1.3); **24.5** natsbus `WithContextPropagation` traceparent lift (consumer-supplied `Propagator` seam, no OTel dep, D4 — rides 24.2's `MetadataFunc`); **24.6** memorybus optional error sink (D5 parity, no durability emulation); **24.7** JetStream `StreamConfig`/`WithJetStream` + `Nats-Msg-Id` broker dedup (opt-in, D1); **24.8** durable consumers + explicit ack via `*Bus.SubscribeWith` (nil→Ack, err→Nak/NakWithDelay redelivery; `Subscribe` frozen/interface-satisfying — spec amended from the impossible variadic-on-`Subscribe`; FIX-131 `Close`-race resolved inline); **24.9** `WithMaxDeliver`/`WithDeadLetter` → DLQ (client-side core-NATS republish of envelope+headers on the final delivery, then `Term`); **24.10** replay/backfill `WithDeliverAll`/`WithDeliverFromTime`/`WithDeliverFromSequence` (consumer start-cursor; un-set = `DeliverAllPolicy` byte-identical default); **24.11** convenience — `Connect(url, ...ConnectOption)` prod-default constructor (`ConnectOption = Option` alias, pairs with `WithJetStream`), `WithActionSubjects()` action-qualified subjects + envelope-version bump to "2", `WithCodec` **dropped** (no proto/msgpack need surfaced).

**24.11 closure sweep (landed 2026-07-31):** `make check` clean across all 33 modules under `-race` (6:07 total; longest leg `cmd/sqlgen/cli` ~340s); `make check-examples` clean across all 13 example modules (1:54 total; longest legs graphql ~40.6s, mysql ~15.3s, postgres ~9.2s); `make test-integration` clean (8:39 total; longest legs `cmd/sqlgen/cli` ~476s, `cmd/sqlgen` ~245s, `cmd/sqlgen/gen` ~45s, `event/natsbus` ~6.5s with the JetStream integration tests actually executing, `cache/redis` ~7.8s); no flakes. **FIX triage:** no open FIXes at closure — FIX-131 (the sole phase-24-origin entry, a pre-existing `Close`-outside-lock race widened by 24.8's `jsConsume` field) was resolved inline during 24.8 with a `-race` guard; 0 carried forward. **Surface delta summary (across Phase 24):** the shipped `event/natsbus` module became a production-grade transport entirely through additive Options — `New(conn)` core mode stays byte-for-byte identical. New producer-side surface is the single additive `event.Config.MetadataFunc` field (D6) plus one generator hook-entry merge point; everything else (JetStream durable mode + broker dedup, `SubscribeWith` durable consumers with ack/nak/backoff/max-deliver/dead-letter, replay/backfill start policies, trace-context propagation, error handlers/retry, `Connect` prod-default constructor, action-qualified subjects) is natsbus-local with its own `go.mod`. The stdlib-only `event` contract (interfaces, `SubscribeOptions`) and every generated `event_hooks_gen.go` are undisturbed; memorybus gained only an optional local error sink. Wire form is stabilized (snake_case `json` tags, `Sqlgen-Envelope-Version` header). **Two optional non-blocking follow-ups noted by 24.11 auto-review** (not FIX-logged): soften the PRD §28.7.1 `WithCodec` bullet to read "deferred" to match the drop; and a tracked note on the pre-existing "single-table subscribe assumes a schema token" invariant (load-bearing for empty-schema/SQLite setups). **Next:** **Phase 25 (GraphQL Read Surface Completion) — all 13 of 13 sub-items complete, awaiting the closure sweep; broken down 2026-08-25** — see below. Ticket B is closed (25.6 → 25.7, the F1 cross-tenant view read hole). **Ticket C is closed: 25.8 landed 2026-09-04** and carried the view GraphQL emission with it; **25.9 landed 2026-09-04** as the proof layer — a tenanted view and a materialized view on the `graphql` example, the F1+F3 two-tenant E2E over the real gqlgen server (verified failing-first), the §25.1 query-count pin on a tenanted view, the enum/JSONB filter E2E, and the matview read-surface + no-`Refresh` check. **Ticket D is closed (25.10 → 25.11 → 25.12, all 2026-09-04). 25.10 landed** the F8/D11 runtime prerequisite. `sql/` gained a structured `Exists{CorrelationColumn, Subquery}` condition value (`sql.Exists(col, subquery)` constructor, mirroring the `And`/`Or` shape) plus `sql.CorrelationToken`, the marker a generated subquery uses for its parent-side reference. The qualifier is threaded internally through `appendWhere` → `buildWhere` → `expandCondition` → `expandComposition` and resolved by each builder from what its own `FROM` clause says — `SelectOptions.Alias` in `BuildSelectJoin`, `FormatTable(t)` in `BuildSelect`, `BuildCount`, `BuildExists`, `BuildUpdate`, `BuildIncrement`, `BuildSoftDelete`, `BuildHardDelete` (the last five matter because a relationship filter reaches them all through `ToConditions`). No public builder signature changed, `sql/` stayed stdlib-only, every pre-existing `sql` test passed untouched, and **nothing emits the condition yet, so there is no golden churn** — 25.11 is where it gets used. Verified failing-first: collapsing the join qualifier to `FormatTable` fails the new tests in 17 places. **25.11** emitted the member and the correlated `EXISTS`; **25.12** put it on the GraphQL surface as a nested input plus translator recursion, both driven by one context slice, with the member gated on the target's own API exposure. **Ticket A is closed: 25.2 landed 2026-09-04** — the D2 completeness lint on both surfaces in both directions, plus the relationship-member arm carried in from 25.12, wired into `buildOneAPITable` / `buildOneAPIView` so it hard-errors under `sqlgen validate` as well as `sqlgen generate`. Its one open decision resolved as **drop the filter field**: the `StringComparator` fallback is deleted, so the two comparator shapes with no sound GraphQL face are absent from both surfaces rather than accepted-and-ignored. Zero golden churn across all 13 examples. **All 13 sub-items are complete — Phase 25 is ready for `/close-phase 25`.**

**Phase 26 (Standard Library UUID as First-Class) — all 7 sub-items complete; 26.0–26.3a 2026-09-11, 26.4 + 26.5 shipped together 2026-09-15 as the breaking pair.** **The default is flipped:** a `uuid` column with no configuration resolves to the standard library's `uuid.UUID`, nullable to `*uuid.UUID` (`nullPtrOnly` — the package ships no `NullUUID`, and `use_pointers: false` does not change it). Consumer-visible: nullable UUID GraphQL fields move from the `NullUUID` scalar to `UUID`, and the `MarshalNullUUID` / `UnmarshalNullUUID` pair stops being emitted for stdlib-bound packages; filter inputs are unchanged (`NullableIDComparator` either way); nullable UUID columns lose `.Valid` / `.UUID` in favour of `!= nil` / `*v`, which the compiler catches at every site. **Example tree:** five examples on the standard library (`postgres`, `graphql`, `graphql_top_level`, `tenancy`, `tenancy_postgres`), `postgres_stdlib` held on `github.com/google/uuid` as the wrapper-path guard (its table-scoped override **promoted to global** — post-flip, holding a wrapper means holding it for the whole package), and `graphql_null_wrappers` moved to `github.com/gofrs/uuid/v5`, closing a gap where no example exercised `uuidgofrs` at all. **Two live defects surfaced the moment a non-stdlib binding was actually exercised.** (1) the GraphQL scalar template hardcoded `uuid.Parse`, which gofrs does not export (`FromString` is its string constructor). Fixed per-integration on 26.3a's existing seam, with a PRD amendment first — §7.4 gained a **"Parsing a UUID from a string"** table and §26.4.1's registry row stopped naming one library's spelling. The three `uuidgen_compile_*` pins now compile the parse call against the real library. (2) The `graphql` example's `order_items.external_ref` is a **TEXT** column retyped to `uuid.UUID` via `column_map`; the standard library's `uuid.UUID` ships neither `sql.Scanner` nor `driver.Valuer` and pgx keys `UUIDCodec` on the `uuid` OID, so that retype cannot scan — both wrapper-backed integrations do ship `Scan`/`Value`, so it works under either of them. §7.4's no-Scanner claim was scoped to `uuid` columns and the FIX-156 fixture moved to `graphql_null_wrappers` (GraphQL-enabled and gofrs-bound; `postgres_stdlib` has no `api:` block, so it could not carry the gqlgen-handoff half). **Not yet run:** `make test-integration` under Docker (deferred to `/verify` by user direction; `make check` and `make check-examples` are both clean) and the `/close-phase 26` sweep.

**Phase 26 (Standard Library UUID as First-Class) — In Progress; 26.0, 26.1, 26.2, 26.3 and 26.3a complete 2026-09-11.** Broken down 2026-09-11, 6 sub-items plus **26.3a**, added mid-phase when two blockers surfaced — 7 in total. Go 1.27 ships a `uuid` package in the standard library; the phase makes it the binding a `uuid` column resolves to with no configuration and demotes `github.com/google/uuid` / `github.com/gofrs/uuid/v5` to supported overrides. Plan: `docs/design/archive/UUID_MIGRATION.md` (every behavioral claim measured against `go1.27.1`, `pgx v5.9.1`, `modernc.org/sqlite v1.48.1` and PostgreSQL 16; method in §11). **The `Valuer`/`Scanner` question is settled and is not a blocker** — `database/sql` special-cases `uuid.UUID` in both directions and pgx routes it through the same `[16]byte` → `byte16Wrapper` → `UUIDCodec` path `google/uuid` has always taken; verified on both `output.driver` values across all five `QueryExecMode` settings, `uuid[]`, `SendBatch`, `CopyFrom`, `RETURNING`, and `CollectRows`. **Sub-items:** **26.0** PRD sync (§7.2 default, §7.3 `nullPtrOnly`, §7.4 integration tables, §26.4.1 `NullUUID` scalar gating, §4.13 validation row) — *blocks all*; **26.1** `uuidstd` integration package (additive); **26.2** reject two UUID libraries in one generated package; **26.3** fix the pre-existing `detectIntegrations` global-vs-table asymmetry; **26.4** flip the default (`"uuid": goString` → `goUUID`, `kind: nullPtrOnly`) — **breaking**; **26.5** migrate examples. **Structural fact driving 26.2:** two UUID libraries cannot coexist in one generated package under either `output.layout` — imports resolve per package, so `file_per_table` fails identically to `single_file`, producing both a name collision and a silent mis-resolution where a google-backed table's `uuid.NullUUID` binds against the standard library. The existing `stdsql` aliasing does not generalize (both packages are named `uuid` and both export `UUID`), so 26.2 converts the case to a config error and **must land before 26.4**. **Example coverage decision:** two non-stdlib guards, not one — `postgres_stdlib` holds `google/uuid`, `graphql_null_wrappers` moves to `gofrs/uuid/v5`, closing a gap where no example exercised `uuidgofrs` at all; the two are not redundant because the three integrations spell the zero UUID three different ways (`uuid.UUID{}`, the `uuid.Nil` variable, and — for the stdlib — neither, its `Nil` being a function). **Reversibility:** JSON and cache payloads are byte-identical across the migration, so no cache flush is required; the published GraphQL schema is the only one-way surface. **Dependency order:** 26.0 → {26.1, 26.2, 26.3} in parallel → 26.4 (needs 26.1 + 26.2) → 26.5. 26.4 + 26.5 are the breaking release and ship together. **26.2 landed 2026-09-11** as a resolution-level (phase 3) rule in `cmd/sqlgen/gen/uuid_library.go`, reached from one shared entry point (`validateResolvedPackage`) that `generate`, `validate` and `graphql gen` all call. It counts the distinct import paths behind every column, view column and `extras` field whose resolved Go type is qualified `uuid.`, and rejects more than one, naming both paths, a selecting entity for each, and the separate-`output.dir` remedy. Keying on the resolved qualifier rather than on a fixed list of the three known import paths also catches a fork or vendored copy. Zero golden churn — every example declares at most one UUID library, so the rule is a no-op across the tree today. **Open question for 26.3:** PRD §4.13 and §7.4 both name `views.<view>.overrides.types`, and no such config key exists (`ViewConfig` has no `Overrides` field; the JSON schema's `view` definition has 7 properties to the table's 20). Views reach a UUID type only through the global override, which 26.2 does collect — but 26.3's task list names the same non-existent key, so either the PRD sentence is wrong or the config is missing a field, and that call belongs to 26.3.

**26.0 (PRD sync) complete 2026-09-11 — docs only, `docs/PRD.md` the sole file changed.** §7.2 retypes `uuid` → `uuid.UUID` / `*uuid.UUID` and `uuid[]` → `[]uuid.UUID`, with the Array type rule rewritten to key on the base type's **resolved** mapping (the base always resolves non-null, so the element never carries a `sql.Null*` or pointer shape) since `uuid[]` is no longer an override-only example. §7.3 gains the `nullPtrOnly` paragraph — `uuid.UUID` joins `time.Duration` and `net.IPNet`, and `use_pointers: false` does **not** produce a wrapper for that class; the integration choice is the lever, not the flag. §7.4's UUID subsection is rewritten around one integration table with the stdlib first (import value / Go type / zero value / nullable Go type), adding the stdlib's three normative properties (no null wrapper; no `Valuer`/`Scanner` and none needed, with the `database/sql` special-case and the pgx `[16]byte` → `byte16Wrapper` → `UUIDCodec` route, plus never-scan-into-`pgtype.UUID`; `comparable`), the GraphQL consequence, google/gofrs as **alternative integrations** retaining the `uuid.NullUUID` pairing, the three zero-value spellings as the reason `ZeroValue` is per-integration, and the **one-library-per-generated-package** rule with its per-package (not per-file) import-resolution cause and the separate-`output.dir` remedy. §26.4.1 gates the `NullUUID` registry entry to the wrapper-backed integrations — derived from the existing resolved-`nullable.type` rule rather than special-cased — and records that the registry keys on the bare Go type strings, so one entry pair serves either wrapper and **cannot distinguish them**, sound only while the §7.4 rule holds. §4.13 gains the resolution-level (phase 3) row for 26.2. Four consistency edits outside the five named sections (§1 `go.mod` comment, §3 package map `gotype/uuidstd/`, §8.1 array test bullet, §26.4 binding table gaining a `*uuid.UUID` → `UUID` row) are itemized in the 26.0 completion record. One old §7.4 bullet — "UUID v4 and v7 generation support using the selected library's generator" — was **removed rather than rewritten**, because it is false today; §8.6 still carries the `generation.uuid_version` requirement. Anchor validation: 526 headings, 397 internal links, 0 broken; `make vet test` clean across all modules (`make check`'s lint leg fails on a **pre-existing environment drift** — golangci-lint 2.12.2 is built with go1.26.2 and cannot typecheck the go1.27.1 stdlib, failing inside GOROOT's `math/rand/v2`; reproduced with the diff stashed, and the repo still has no `.tool-versions` pin). **The auto-review pass caught five real under-reach inconsistencies, all fixed inline** — §9.8.3's FK map-key table guarded `*uuid.UUID` with a `.Valid` it does not have (split into pointer and wrapper rows), §11.2's `String`-fallback prose still listed `uuid.UUID` as an override-only text-storing `driver.Valuer` type, §11.2 had **no** `uuid` comparator entry at all (added the SQL-type-keyed `ID`/`NullableID` rule 26.4 needs to cite), §9.8.4's create snippet used gofrs's two-value `uuid.NewV4()`, and §7.4 overstated the per-file import union. A sixth — 26.3 having nothing to cite — was closed by adding §7.4's “Integration detection is scope-independent”. A seventh was **escalated rather than fixed**: §28.8 normatively mandates `google/uuid` for event IDs and names a `uuid.Must` the stdlib lacks, so it contradicts §7.4 outright — amending it either way *is* the B-1 decision, so it was folded into B-1. **Two blockers were raised, both measured against `go1.27.1` and the generator at HEAD, neither covered by `docs/design/archive/UUID_MIGRATION.md` nor by any sub-item as broken down** (full write-up in the phase file's "Open Blockers" section): **B-1** — `context_event.go` hardcodes `github.com/google/uuid` into every event-hooks file regardless of config (the `cache` example proves it: no `uuid` override, yet the import is there), so 26.5's migration of `tenancy`, `graphql` and `tenancy_postgres` would put two UUID libraries in one generated package — the exact failure 26.2 exists to prevent — and 26.2's specified collection sites (`overrides.types`, `column_map.<col>.import`) would not even see it; **B-2** — `funcPKAutoGenType` emits `uuid.Must(uuid.NewV7())` for v7 app-strategy PKs, but the stdlib `uuid` package exports no `Must` and no `NewString` and its `NewV7()` returns a bare `UUID`, so that spelling does not compile once 26.4 flips the default (and is already wrong for gofrs, unnoticed because no example exercises it). No example uses an app-strategy UUID PK, so the golden suite will not catch B-2. **Both were resolved by user decision on 2026-09-11: fix the hardcoded event-ID library to follow the package's selected UUID integration, and fix the `uuid.Must(...)` spellings as part of this migration.** That made their PRD side 26.0's business, so a second round of normative text landed: **§7.4 gained "Generating UUID values"**, a per-integration table of the v4/v7 calls in both value and string forms, every cell measured against `go1.27.1`, `github.com/google/uuid` and `github.com/gofrs/uuid/v5 v5.5.1` (gofrs fetched into a scratch module, being in no module graph yet) — the surfaces disagree more than the old code assumed: only google exports `New()` and `NewString()`, `Must` exists in google and gofrs but not the stdlib, and the stdlib's constructors return a bare `UUID` where the others return `(UUID, error)`; the stdlib v4 cell is `uuid.NewV4()` rather than `uuid.New()` because `New` is not contractually v4. **§28.8 was rewritten** to specify the selected integration's string-form call and the one-library-per-package guarantee, replacing text that mandated `google/uuid` outright; **§8.6 and §9.8.4** now cite the table instead of spelling calls of their own. The code work is the new sub-item **26.3a** (per-integration UUID generation expressions), placed between 26.3 and 26.4 because it **must land before both** — 26.5 migrates three events-enabled examples to the stdlib and would otherwise emit two UUID imports in one generated package. It also fixes two live defects: events-enabled packages are no longer forced to carry `github.com/google/uuid`, and a gofrs-bound package with events can compile at all for the first time. **26.1 (`uuidstd` integration package) complete 2026-09-11.** New `cmd/sqlgen/gotype/uuidstd/` (`ImportPath = "uuid"`, `SQLTypes() = ["uuid"]`, `Override()` with `Type: "uuid.UUID"` / `ZeroValue: "uuid.UUID{}"` and **no** `Nullable`), registered first in `knownIntegrations`. Every acceptance criterion was already satisfied by existing machinery — verified by reading it first — so the integration itself is declaration-only. **The verification task found a real defect and fixing it was in scope:** `enrichOverride` back-filled `Nullable` across libraries, returning `uuid.NullUUID` for a stdlib-bound column **200/200** in a probe (deterministic, not a race). Root cause is the pairing in `detectIntegrations` — it enriches whatever global override sits at each of an integration's own `sqlTypes` without checking the override names that library, and all three UUID integrations claim `uuid`, so a second UUID library anywhere in the overrides wrote its wrapper onto the first one's import (`uuid.NullUUID` beside `import "uuid"`, which has no such identifier). Order-independent: whichever integration carries a non-empty `Nullable` wins. The guard went into `enrichOverride` rather than the call site **deliberately, so 26.3's table- and view-scoped detection inherits it**; an override naming no import is not a competing claim and stays eligible, preserving the existing `numeric`-declares-`decimal` shorthand. Failing-first verified. **A second defect at the same call site, found by the auto-review: nondeterministic generated output.** The sibling `else` branch registers an integration for any SQL type the user did not override, with no ownership check, and `detected` is a **map** — so with two UUID libraries in play registration was a last-writer-wins race over iteration order. Measured: a nullable `uuid` column resolved `*uuid.UUID` **151/200** runs and `uuid.NullUUID` **49/200** from one config. Fixed by iterating in sorted import-path order, restoring PRD §5.7 determinism without deciding which library *should* win for an unclaimed type (26.2 rejects that config); failing-first at 10/10. Taken here because 26.1 adds the third claimant on `uuid` and the fix is three lines in a function this sub-item already touches. The deeper ownership question is routed to 26.2, and `enrichOverride` never filling `Import` on an import-less override to 26.3. **Golden byte-identity: all 12 examples byte-identical.** Under Go 1.27 only 9 are reachable — the three gqlgen-invoking examples (`graphql`, `graphql_top_level`, `mysql`) fail identically on HEAD, as does `make check-examples` in `graphql/tests` — but under `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.0` the full suite passes **all 12** with 26.1's changes in place, leaving the tree clean. **That gqlgen break is diagnosed and RESOLVED (2026-09-11) — it was not sqlgen's bug.** gqlgen exits 1 with empty stdout *and* stderr because `main.go` routes `log` to `io.Discard` without `--verbose` and the failure arrives as a `log.Fatalf`; with `--verbose` it reads `package "github.com/vektah/gqlparser/v2/ast" without types`. That Fatalf is `x/tools/go/packages.go:1214`, reached because gqlgen v0.17.90's loader mode (`internal/code/packages.go:15-20`) omits `NeedDeps`/`NeedImports`, and **under Go 1.27** x/tools v0.42.0 stops populating dependency types for that mode. Isolated in a ~25-line program using only x/tools with gqlgen's exact mode — adding `NeedDeps|NeedImports` fixes it, and holding everything constant but the toolchain gives `go1.26.0` → complete, `go1.27.1` → Fatalf. An earlier diagnosis blaming the root `go.work` was **wrong and is retracted** (`e2e_test.go:134` already sets `GOWORK=off`); a 44 GB `GOCACHE` clear changes nothing. **CI is green today** only because every workflow installs Go from `go-version-file` and every `go.mod` says `go 1.26.0`. **This blocks 26.4/26.5**, which need Go 1.27 for the stdlib `uuid` package and must regenerate the gqlgen goldens: raising the `go` directive moves CI onto the toolchain where gqlgen dies. **Fixed by upgrading gqlgen v0.17.90 → v0.17.95** across the five example modules requiring it, which carries **x/tools v0.42.0 → v0.49.0**. The repair is x/tools', not gqlgen's — v0.17.95's loader mode is byte-identical and still omits `NeedDeps`; the isolated probe shows v0.49.0 loading cleanly where v0.42.0 fatals, all else held constant. A future gqlgen bump that lowers its x/tools floor would regress this. v0.17.95 needs `go 1.26`, already satisfied, so no go-directive bump was required and the fix is independent of the pending Go 1.27 work. Churn: 146 generated files, **all gqlgen-owned — no sqlgen output moved** — plus 5 `go.mod`/`go.sum` pairs; upstream deltas are deferred-field `FieldSetView` handling and `_, _ = fmt.Fprint(...)` in enum `MarshalGQL`. `TestE2EGoldenFiles` now passes **all 12 examples under Go 1.27.1**, tree clean, and `make check` is green; the `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.0` workaround is no longer needed. The guard provably cannot fire in any example: each of the 12 configs declares at most one distinct UUID import path, and none names `import: uuid`. **Next:** 26.2, 26.3 or 26.3a — all unblocked and mutually independent.

**26.3 (`detectIntegrations` global-vs-table asymmetry) complete 2026-09-11.** `cmd/sqlgen/gotype/gotype.go` + the new white-box `cmd/sqlgen/gotype/scope_test.go` + black-box additions to `gotype_test.go`, plus the PRD amendment below — three Go files, no generated output moved. `detectIntegrations` ran once in `NewResolver` over `r.globalOverrides` and nothing else, so a table-scoped map reached step 2 of the chain raw: a `github.com/google/uuid` override with no `nullable:` gave `uuid.NullUUID` globally and `*uuid.UUID` under `tables.<t>:`, with nothing in the consumer's config to explain the difference. The fix is **one shared detector** — `detectIntegrationsIn` is now the single implementation both scopes call, so they cannot drift apart again (the reasoning 26.2 used for `validateResolvedPackage`) — and `scopeIntegrations`, the table-scoped twin of `detectIntegrations`, which splits its result the same two ways: the scope's own entries enriched (step 2) and what the integration contributes for SQL types the scope did not spell out (step 4, ahead of the global integrations). Enrichment is `enrichOverride` reused verbatim, so **26.1's cross-library guard is inherited rather than re-implemented** — which is exactly why 26.1 put it inside the function instead of at its call site. Two deliberate choices: the scope map is **cloned, never mutated** (`r.globalOverrides` is the resolver's own map, but a table's is the parsed config shared with every other reader — writing through it would make resolution depend on which table was built first), and detection is **recomputed per call, not cached** (a scope selecting no integration — every table-scoped block in the tree today — returns the caller's own map with nothing allocated). **"Activates that integration" was read to include the integration's other SQL types**, the one thing past the acceptance criteria: shopspring claims both `numeric` and `decimal`, and leaving that half out would have fixed one asymmetry while leaving its twin in the same function; §7.4 now spells out the consequence and the step-5 rank. **Golden churn zero, and structural rather than lucky** — an audit through the real config loader enumerated every table-scoped `overrides.types` block in the tree (4 tables across `mysql` / `postgres` / `postgres_stdlib` / `sqlite`, plus two `graphql` tables naming no integration): **every** entry naming a known integration already declares `nullable:` explicitly, and **every** newly activated sibling names a SQL type no column in that table uses (checked against the schemas — `mysql.warehouses` is `DECIMAL` with no `NUMERIC`, `postgres`/`postgres_stdlib` are `NUMERIC` with no `DECIMAL`, `sqlite` is `REAL` with neither).
