Failing-first measured: stubbing `scopeIntegrations` to `return scope, nil` fails **21 subtests** and leaves the three negative controls passing. **The PRD moved, not the config.** 26.2's flagged contradiction — §4.13 and §7.4 both naming `views.<view>.overrides.types`, a key `ViewConfig`, `schema/v1.json` and `context_view.go` all say does not exist — was put to the user rather than guessed (project rule 1); the answer was to remove it from the PRD, so §4.13 now names the two real scopes and records that view columns participate through the **global** binding (which 26.2's rule already collects), and §7.4 states that the two scopes are the only ones and points per-view type control at a column's `@type` annotation. **One adjacent asymmetry was found and deliberately left standing:** `registerWrappersFromOverrides` also walks only the global map, so a **user-declared** Null wrapper at table scope never reaches `r.userWrappers` — live in `postgres.warehouses.region_name` (`customtypes.OptionalString`, non-default `Val`/`Set`) but inert, since that column is a plain domain with no `REFERENCES` and no O2M loader asks for its extraction. It is not a variation on this fix: `userWrappers` is resolver state read by `DeriveScalarExtraction(goType string)`, which has no scope argument, so the repair is a `NewResolver` signature change (96 test call sites) or a new entry point every construction site must remember — the FIX-162 shape. **Auto-review: three findings, all three closed in the same pass.** A stale comment in `gen/uuid_library.go` still quoted the §4.13 list with view scope in it (fixed inline); the new §7.4 sentence **overclaimed** — unqualified "identical override text resolves to an identical mapping at both scopes" is true for integrations but not for a user-declared wrapper, so it was narrowed to the integration claim rather than left asserting a property the code lacks (and a draft cross-reference to §7.5 was dropped on finding §7.5 says nothing about scope); and 26.1's routed question — "`enrichOverride` never fills `Import` on an import-less override" — was **answered by measurement**: that shape resolves to `Import: ""` *identically at both scopes*, so it is not an asymmetry and 26.3's parity claim is untouched, but a pre-existing hole in config validation — `validateColumnTypeLiterals` rejects a package-qualified literal with no import for `type_map` and `column_map.<col>.type`, and nothing checks the by-SQL-type `overrides.types.<t>.type`. Not fixed here on purpose: the candidate fix changes the global path too and changes what 26.2's rule counts as a claim. `make check` exit 0 (8 modules, `-race`); `make check-examples` exit 0 (12 example modules under Docker); `TestE2EGoldenFiles` 12/12, tree clean, re-run after the review fixes; PRD anchors 526 headings / 408 links / 0 broken. **Next: 26.3a**, which must land before 26.4 and 26.5.

**26.3a (per-integration UUID generation expressions) complete 2026-09-11 — resolves B-1 and B-2 together, and 26.4/26.5 now have no prerequisites left.** New `cmd/sqlgen/gotype/uuidgen.go` + `uuidgen_test.go` + three compile pins (`uuidgen_compile_{stdlib,google,gofrs}_test.go`), new `cmd/sqlgen/gen/uuid_generation.go` + test; modified the three integration packages, `gen/context.go`, `context_event.go`, `funcmap.go`, `orchestrate.go`, the `create` / `upsert` templates, seven `gen` test files, **12 example goldens**, **21 `go.mod`/`go.work` files** for the Go floor, and four guideline/doc files. **The seam is one function per half and both halves read the same string:** `gotype.UUIDIntegration` carries an import path plus PRD §7.4's four expressions, the spellings live on the integration packages as `V4Expr`/`V7Expr`/`V4StringExpr`/`V7StringExpr` next to the `ImportPath` and `Override()` they already own, `gotype.UUIDIntegrationFor(path)` keys them, `gen.selectUUIDIntegration` answers which one the package chose, and `attachUUIDGeneration` wires it into both call sites. The string forms are **stored, not derived** — google's v4 string form is `uuid.NewString()`, a different call, not `uuid.New()` with `.String()` appended. **Selection is read off the built contexts, not off the resolver — the one departure from the task text, and the departure that makes the acceptance criterion true.** A resolver-side answer can only be "what does the SQL type `uuid` resolve to under the *global* scope", which is wrong in three reachable shapes: a table-scoped `overrides.types.uuid` on a different library than the global one; `column_map.<col>.import`, which never passes through `overrides.types` at all; and **MySQL and SQLite, which declare no `uuid` SQL type**, where a consumer binds `uuid.UUID` through `column_map`/`type_map` on a `CHAR(36)` and the global lookup is empty. So selection reuses 26.2's `collectUUIDClaims`, which already walks every table, view, composite, domain and extras field and reports the import path each *resolved* to — which is what makes the fourth task, "the event-hooks import and the column-resolved import are the same library **by construction**", literally true: one string from one collector, not two computations that happen to agree. **The google v4 event ID changed and the PRD closed the open choice:** the acceptance criterion left `uuid.NewString()` vs `uuid.New().String()` to the implementer, PRD §7.4's table and §28.8 both name `uuid.NewString()`, and project rule 1 decides it — so a google-bound events package is **not** byte-identical and three examples move. **`pkAutoGenType` left the funcmap rather than gaining an argument** — the expression is a pure function of (version, PK Go type, package binding), all known before rendering, so it is now the pre-computed `TableContext.PKAutoGenExpr`, which is the rule TEMPLATES.md §6 already stated; the registry entry is gone and a comment in its place says why. **The import is declared, not inferred** (`attachUUIDGeneration` appends it to an app-strategy table's `Imports`, like `arrayScanImports` does for `lib/pq`), which matters most for a PK overridden to a Go `string`, where no column resolves to a uuid-qualified type. **An unknown UUID library keeps its own import and takes the standard library's spellings** — falling back to the stdlib *import* would put a second package named `uuid` in the package, the exact failure the one-library rule exists to prevent; strictly better than before (google's import *and* google's spelling), still a guess, and recorded as one. **A post-build pass is a footgun, so both guidelines now name it:** `PKAutoGenExpr` cannot be filled while one table context is being built, so it joins `wireRelationshipFKMetadata` and `attachTenancyToTables` as a pass running after `buildAllContexts`; a hand-built fixture that omits it renders `pkValue = ` and does not parse, which is how it surfaced (three `reserved_fields_pin_test.go` probe shapes broke). TEMPLATES.md §6 gained a wiring rule and TESTING.md §11 the matching fixture rule, modelled on the FK-metadata rule that exists for the same reason. **Failing-first measured in both halves rather than asserted.** With `context_event.go` reverted, `TestGenerate_StdlibEventsPackageImportsOneUUIDLibrary` reports two UUID libraries in one package — the shape 26.5 would have produced three times over — and four of six `TestBuildEventHooksContext_PerIntegration` subtests fail on the expression; with `uuidGenExpr` reverted to the google-shaped table, `TestGenerate_PKAutoGenExprPerIntegration` fails on **stdlib/v4, stdlib/v7, gofrs/v4**, with google/v4, google/v7 and gofrs/v7 as the negative controls. **The gofrs compile test is a real compile**, and it covers the other two: each pin binds one library to the local name `uuid` (legal because import names are file-scoped, and the only way to spell all three the way a generated file does) and declares `[]uuid.UUID{…}` / `[]string{…}` literals holding that library's four cells verbatim, so a non-existent spelling fails the **build** and the element types make the value/string split load-bearing. That is what closes "a gofrs-bound events-enabled package compiles — it does not today"; it costs `cmd/sqlgen` two **test-only** requires. **Golden churn: 12 files across six examples — two more than the sub-item predicted.** The task named `cache`/`tenancy`/`graphql`/`tenancy_postgres`; the real set is every `events.enabled: true` example, which also includes **`events`** and **`tenancy_mysql`**. It splits two ways: the three with no `uuid` column move from `github.com/google/uuid` + `uuid.New().String()` to `import "uuid"` + `uuid.NewV4().String()`, and the three google-bound ones move `uuid.New().String()` → `uuid.NewString()`. Only `event_hooks_gen.go` moved in each. **`postgres_stdlib` is byte-clean and is the load-bearing negative** — the tree's one app-strategy UUID PK, google-bound through a table-scoped override, so `pkValue = uuid.NewString()` is both the old hardcoded spelling and the new resolved one; the create/upsert path is covered by a real example *and* unchanged. **The three events-only examples no longer require a UUID module** — `github.com/google/uuid` was a direct require in `cache`, `events` and `tenancy_mysql` for exactly one reason (the hardcoded import) and is now `// indirect`; moved by hand rather than by `go mod tidy`, which also pulled a dozen unrelated version bumps. **The Go 1.27 floor moved, by user decision, repo-wide.** This sub-item forces it: the three events-only examples now emit `import "uuid"`, and **`go vet` rejects the standard library `uuid` package in a module declaring `go 1.26.0`** (*"uuid.NewV4 requires go1.27 or later"*) where `go build` alone accepts it. Measured in an isolated two-file module, not inferred — and the workspace's `go` directive does **not** raise a member module's language version, so every module needs its own line. `make check-examples` runs `vet` and `test`, so this was fatal rather than cosmetic. Given a minimal bump or the full move, **the user chose the full move**: all eight `go.mod`s, `go.work`, and all 12 example `go.mod`s read `go 1.27.0`, matching PRD §1; CI installs Go per module from `go-version-file`, so the whole matrix moves with it. No `go.sum` needed changing. **Sweeps:** `make check` exit 0 (8 modules, `-race`, lint `0 issues.` per module, `fmt` left the tree untouched); two lint findings fixed rather than suppressed (staticcheck `QF1011` on the compile pins' `var _ T = expr` form, rewritten as typed slice literals; `prealloc` on an append loop); `TestE2EGoldenFiles` 12/12 byte-clean, tree clean; all 12 example modules build and `go vet` clean under `GOWORK=off` at the new floor. The `.tool-versions` pin warning from 26.0/26.2/26.3 still stands. **Auto-review — one real FAIL fixed inline, three notes, two acted on and one routed.** PRD §8.1.3's funcmap registry table still listed `pkAutoGenType` (TEMPLATES.md had been updated and the PRD had not) — fixed in place using the "…is not registered" form the Naming row already established, with the reason stated. `ValidateGeneration`'s wiring comment claimed to mirror the generate path "in the same order", which stopped being true when `attachUUIDGeneration` joined `buildAllContexts` — reworded to name the one pass left out and why it is unobservable there. The PK matrix ran under `single_file` only and `sqlite` — the tree's one `file_per_table` example — has neither events nor an app-strategy PK, so the import half had no `file_per_table` coverage; the matrix gained a third axis (3 integrations × 2 versions × 2 layouts = 12 subtests). **Routed, not fixed:** an import-less `overrides.types.uuid` silently selects the standard library for the event hooks where the hardcoded import previously bound google — `collectUUIDClaims` skips a resolved `uuid.UUID` with an empty `Import`. This is the same validation hole 26.3 measured and deliberately left standing (`overrides.types.<sql_type>.type` is checked by nothing, unlike the two per-column forms `validateColumnTypeLiterals` covers), it is benign once 26.4 flips the default, and fixing it is a config-validation change to a shipped rule rather than a drive-by — it belongs in a `tracked` FIX if `/verify` agrees. **One note for 26.5:** its example list should be re-read against the six-example churn set — `events` and `tenancy_mysql` are events-enabled and were not in its plan, though neither has a `uuid` column so neither needs a `sqlgen.yml` edit. **`/verify 26.3a` ran 2026-09-14 and logged two `blocking` FIXes, so the sub-item does not pass the `/done` gate yet.** **FIX-201** — `tenancy.type` is a **fourth UUID import surface** `collectUUIDClaims` does not read (it walks columns, view columns, composites, domains and extras only), and this sub-item turned it from a silently-agreeing hardcode into a package that does not compile: with `events.enabled: true`, `tenancy.type` naming google, an app-strategy `uuid` PK and no `overrides.types.uuid`, `sqlgen generate` exits **0** and `models_gen.go` imports both `"uuid"` (declared by `attachUUIDGeneration` after selection found no claims) and `"github.com/google/uuid"` (from tenancy) → `uuid redeclared in this block`. Confirmed a **regression** by generating the same config with the pre-26.3a binary at `f463493`, which emits google alone and compiles; 26.4 does not fix it, and it is live for 26.5's **`tenancy`** and **`graphql`** migrations — the only two examples declaring a `tenancy.type`, both escaping today only because their global `overrides.types` supplies an agreeing google column claim (`blob` in `tenancy`, `uuid` in `graphql`). **FIX-202** — the repo-wide Go 1.27 floor **breaks CI's lint job on every module**: `lint.yml:24` and `examples.yml:48` pin `golangci-lint v2.10.1`, built with go1.26.0, which now refuses outright (*"the Go language version (go1.26) used to build golangci-lint is lower than the targeted Go version (1.27.0)"*, reproduced with the pinned release binary); the local toolchain moved to 2.13.2 during 26.0 and the workflow pins never followed, which is the drift the still-missing `.tool-versions` keeps enabling. **FIX-203** (`tracked`) is the import-less `overrides.types.uuid` item 26.3a routed for `/verify` to adjudicate. Everything else verified PASS, including all 12 PRD §7.4 cells (pinned twice — literally, and as compiled code against the three real libraries), the §28.8 by-construction guarantee for every surface selection *does* read, the 12-file golden churn with `postgres_stdlib` byte-clean, and the Go floor's completeness (all 20 `go.mod` + `go.work`). **Next: resolve FIX-201 and FIX-202, then 26.4 + 26.5**, the breaking pair, which ship together.

**25.11 landed 2026-09-04** — D10's payoff, and the sub-item that carries the golden churn 25.10 deferred. Every list relationship now contributes a `*<Target>Filter` member to the parent filter, compiled to a correlated `EXISTS` built from the join metadata already on `RelationshipContext` (FK for O2M; junction + both FKs for M2M, as a single `EXISTS` over the junction joined to the target, not two queries). To-one relationships emit no member (§11.1). The target's **own** scoping goes inside the subquery: its soft-delete default (§17.3, skipped when the caller set the comparator or the target's `exclude_deleted` is off) and, when tenanted, its tenant predicate (§29.4.1) — without the second a relationship filter is a cross-tenant existence probe. **One decision was escalated to the user before any code was written**, because the PRD does not resolve it: §11.1 shows the subquery's tenant as a *bound parameter* (`tgt."workspace_id" = $3`) while pinning `ToConditions` to a signature with no ctx, no client, and no tenant. The user chose the **variadic `FilterOption`** over a required scope parameter or correlating to the parent's tenant column; PRD §11.1 gained a `FilterOption` subsection recording it. The parameter is emitted only when the package has a relationship filter member, which is why four of the twelve examples (`cache`, `events`, `tenancy_mysql`, `tenancy_postgres`) regenerate **byte-identically**. The option set also carries the **subquery nesting depth**, which names the target alias per level (`tgt0`/`jn0`, `tgt1`/…) — a self-referential filter nested inside itself would otherwise let the inner subquery shadow the reference its own correlation resolves against and silently compare the related row to itself. `sql/` gained one helper, `SubqueryWhere`, which renders the target's conditions as an alias-qualified, bare-`$` fragment (via a `bareDialect` placeholder wrapper) for `Exists` to renumber; passing the alias down as the qualifier is what makes a nested `EXISTS` correlate to its own level. Tenant resolution reaches all eight filter-accepting client methods through a per-client `filterOptions` helper, gated on a **transitive** reachability flag over the relationship-filter graph. Shapes `sql.Exists` cannot express emit nothing rather than something half-formed: composite-PK parents, M2M targets without a single-column PK, targets outside the generated set, and field-name collisions with a column comparator. **MySQL 1093** (25.10's sharpest carry-forward) is resolved as documented-not-prevented: a self-referential relationship filter is read-path-only on MySQL, stated in PRD §11.1 and on every generated helper. E2E across real PostgreSQL, MySQL and SQLite plus eleven tenancy-example tests, two of them written failing-first (the soft-delete case asserts the filter matches *before* deleting; the cross-tenant case confirms the drifted row is reachable as tenant B). **Auto-review found one blocking issue, fixed inline and pinned with a failing-first render test**: a shared (untenanted) parent whose list target is tenanted still has to resolve a tenant, but carried no tenant Go type, so the client rendered `tenancy.TenantResolver[]`; the gen fixture already produced that shape but nothing rendered the client template for it, so both suites stayed green over broken output. Three lesser findings were also fixed inline — the static `filter:` predicate is now `qualifyFilter`-qualified at codegen (a bare column is ambiguous inside the M2M subquery, the FIX-098 hazard), a `$` in a static filter is now a codegen error rather than a silent arg shift, and `ToConditions` joined the reserved relationship-name set. **25.12 landed the same day** and closed Ticket D; **25.2** (Ticket A's completeness lint) must come last. Open cross-phase carry-forwards remain 12.12/13.11 (lint-rule stretch item, gated on Phase 12 infra) and the manifest JSON Schema v1.0.0 freeze (held at `0.1.0` by user direction).

**25.2 (completeness lint; D2) complete 2026-09-04. Ticket A is closed, and with it the phase's sub-item list.** `ValidateAPIFilterCompleteness` / `ValidateAPISortCompleteness` in the new `cmd/sqlgen/gen/api_lint.go`, both taking `(APIEntity, APITableContext)` — the same signature shape the §26.5.2 walker lint takes, so a **view is linted by the identical code path with no view arm**. Wired into `buildOneAPITable` / `buildOneAPIView` right after the walker lint, which puts them on the `BuildAPIContext` path and therefore under **`sqlgen validate`** as well as `sqlgen generate`. Both report every violation via `errors.Join` rather than the first. **Filter, columns:** schema side derived under the template's own gate (`not $f.PrimaryKey` and `$f.Filterable`) against `FilterFields`, both directions. **Filter, relationships** (the arm carried in from 25.12): the two emitters read one slice, so "the sides disagree" is not expressible there — the lint checks instead that each entry renders **both** halves, names a member the model `<T>Filter` actually has, and collides with neither a column field nor another member. **Sort:** enum values against `SortFields` cases both ways, plus a sortable column that resolved no enum value and a case returning a column the entity does not have (that name goes straight into `ORDER BY`, so it is neither a parse nor a compile error).

**The decision 25.2 owned: drop the fallback, do not hard-error.** The PRD contradicted itself — §26.5.3 makes "absent from both, not exempt from it" the only way a non-projectable column satisfies the lint, while §26.12 documented the custom-scalar-array case as one that *keeps* the `StringComparator` fallback. The user chose **drop the filter field**, and the escape hatch is why: hard-erroring blocks `sqlgen generate` outright on a schema sqlgen handles today, and the only way out is a §32.2 role (`hidden` / `write_only`) that **also** strips the column from the object type — the consumer would trade reads for the ability to generate. Dropping costs a filter that never worked (accepted and silently ignored) and nothing else: the column keeps its object-type field, its sort-enum value, and its member on the **Go** `<T>Filter`. `resolveAPIFilterProjection`'s `else` arm and `fallbackComparatorInput` are gone; the two shapes that reached it — a `comparator.Enum[T]` whose `T` is not a schema enum, and a `comparator.Slice[T]` whose element is outside the five measured built-ins — now return a zero projection. **`APIFieldContext.Filterable` is now read back off the projection** (`InputTypeName != ""`), so the template's gate and the projection are one fact; `APIFilterProjection` consequently satisfies a **biconditional** (input type and translator populated together or empty together) — the symmetric form 25.1's completion record flagged as unsatisfiable at the time, now asserted on every row of `TestFilterProjection_perGoType`.

**Verified failing-first at two levels.** Stubbing both lint bodies to `return nil` fails 11 of the 15 new tests (the four survivors are the baseline and the three negative cases, which assert silence — correct). More importantly, re-introducing the `StringComparator` fallback makes **`BuildAPIContext` itself** hard-error: `api: filter for table "products" emits column "seen_at" as GraphQL field "seenAt" with no entry in the filter translator … PRD §26.5.3`. That is simultaneously proof the lint fires on real generated output, proof it sits on the codegen hard-error path, and proof the fallback drop was required rather than cosmetic. `make check` clean (33 modules, `-race`); `make check-examples` clean (13 modules); **zero golden churn** — `git status` shows nothing under `cmd/sqlgen/testdata/` after a full regenerate, meeting the acceptance criterion literally. **PRD amended in three places:** §26.12's bullet rewritten from "keeps the `StringComparator` fallback" to "gets no GraphQL filter field at all", now naming both unsound shapes (the second was previously undocumented) and stating what survives; §26.5.3's lint list gained the relationship arm, the `ORDER BY`-column arm, and the validate/view scope sentence; one broken section anchor corrected.

**25.12 (relationship filter GraphQL projection; F4) complete 2026-09-04. Ticket D is closed.** The consumer's blocked story — `tasks(filter: {status: DONE, assignees: {id: {eq: X}}})` — now parses, translates and executes. The whole projection is **one slice**: `APITableContext.FilterRelationships` is built once per table and read by both emitters, so `schema.graphqls.tmpl` renders `<name>: <Target>Filter` and `filter_translate.go.tmpl` renders `out.<Member> = translate<Target>Filter(in.<Member>)` from the same value. That is 25.1's D1 invariant applied to the relationship half, and it makes the F2 failure mode (a field the schema accepts and the translator silently ignores) **unrepresentable** rather than merely absent — there is no second derivation to disagree. Because a nested input is an ordinary gqlgen input and the target's `translate<Target>Filter` already exists for every entity on `APIContext.Tables`, **no new gqlgen machinery was needed anywhere**. The **exposure gate is the security half**: `apiFilterTargets` resolves once for the project which tables a member may name — `api.enabled` plus at least one column surviving §32.2 as readable — and it has to run before the per-table loop because the gate is on the *target*, which may be built after the parent. `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM secrets …)` answers a yes/no question about rows the caller cannot select, so a relationship into a deliberately hidden entity is a read side-channel, not a convenience. (The §32.2 half is unreachable through a validated config — §32.4 keeps PK columns readable — but is implemented and tested because the context builder does not re-run config validation and the PRD states the gate as "excluded from the API".) **One fixture column carries both hard E2E requirements.** The `graphql` example could reach neither a tenanted relationship target nor a self-referential input: its only tenanted tables are `workspace_settings` (composite PK ⇒ no members at all) and `workspace_notes`, and pointing an *untenanted* parent at the latter would have flipped that parent to "must resolve a tenant", turning every previously-unscoped read of it into `ErrMissing` under this example's `tenancy.required: true`. A nullable **self-FK** on `workspace_notes` adds the shape and moves nothing — that table already resolves a tenant on every call — and is simultaneously the §13.5 recursive input (`children: WorkspaceNoteFilter`, an input naming itself). §13.6 circularity came free from the existing `assets ↔ documents` M2M pair, which now emits two inputs naming each other. Termination is a property of the **value**, not the types: gqlgen builds it from a parsed query document, so it is a finite tree — the same property `and`/`or` recursion has relied on since Phase 16. **The `or: [A, B]` trap recorded at 25.4 was pinned here and has since been resolved by FIX-195 (2026-09-10):** writing the union the obvious way returned nothing, because §11.1's `ToConditions` appended one `sql.Or(subConds...)` **per entry**, so entries were ANDed and a union had to be one entry carrying both predicates. The open decision flagged here has been taken — §11.1 is amended so `Or` members OR together and a member's own fields AND together — and `TestRelationshipFilterAPI_RelationshipUnderOr` still asserts both spellings with its two expectations swapped to match. Golden churn is confined to the GraphQL surfaces — 100 files across the five API-enabled examples, and the `filterOptions` plumbing spread to exactly one new client (`workspaceNoteClient`), which is the point of pointing the self-FK at an already-tenanted table. Two proofs verified failing-first: deleting the two template `range` blocks fails five of the ten gen tests, and neutering the tenant injection inside the generated `workspaceNoteChildrenFilterExists` makes the isolation E2E report *"tenant A matched [a-parent] through a child row owned by tenant B"*. **One task could not land and was not dropped:** the tracker asks 25.12 to extend 25.2's filter-completeness lint over relationship members, but `ValidateAPIFilterCompleteness` does not exist yet and 25.2 is scheduled last — the requirement is written into 25.2's task list, and the structural one-derivation guarantee stands in the meantime. **One out-of-scope fix was folded in at the user's direction.** The auto-review noticed `ValidateGeneration` skips every post-build pass `Generate` runs between `BuildTableContexts` and `BuildAPIContext` — the tenancy attaches, the relationship-options dedup, and `wireRelationshipFilters`. Reproduced before deciding: a relationship `filter:` containing a `$` makes `sqlgen generate` hard-error (25.11's guard) while `sqlgen validate` reports **clean**, exactly the class FIX-158 created `ValidateGeneration` to close. Nineteen lines, 25.11-origin, verified failing-first. It also keeps 25.2's deferred relationship-lint arm from no-opping under validate on an always-empty `FilterRelationships`. A companion test asserting the *order* of the mirrored passes was written and **deleted as vacuous** — validate returns only warnings and an error, so the assertion passed with the passes deliberately swapped; the fact is covered on the generate path by 25.11's `TestRelationshipFilters_TargetTenantColumnAttached` instead. Two further carry-forwards: **input-object nesting depth is unbounded** (`api.graphql.max_depth` and gqlgen complexity both count *selection* depth; pre-existing for `and`/`or`, but a relationship level now costs a correlated subquery rather than a paren), and the GraphQL-name collision guard is defensive-only (the model side already refuses the collision by Go field name). `make check` clean across all 33 modules under `-race`; `make check-examples` clean across all 12 example modules.

**Phase 25 (GraphQL Read Surface Completion) — all 13 of 13 sub-items Complete; awaiting the `/close-phase 25` sweep. Broken down 2026-08-25, 25.0 verified complete 2026-08-25, 25.1 complete 2026-08-25, 25.2 complete 2026-09-04, 25.3 verified complete 2026-08-26, 25.4 complete 2026-09-01, 25.5 complete 2026-09-02, 25.6 complete 2026-09-02, 25.7 complete 2026-09-03, 25.8 complete 2026-09-04, 25.9 complete 2026-09-04, 25.10 complete 2026-09-04, 25.11 complete 2026-09-04, 25.12 complete 2026-09-04. All four tickets closed; `/close-phase 25` is the next step.** 13 sub-items (25.0–25.12) across four tickets. Origin: a consumer-side gap report written while verifying the generated read surface through the GraphQL HTTP seam for the first time, **re-verified against sqlgen source** — the four reported gaps all reproduce, and the verification pass found five more (F5–F9). Design doc `docs/design/archive/GRAPHQL_READ_SURFACE.md` (PROPOSED — findings F1–F9, decision record D1–D10, tickets A–D).

**The four reported gaps.** **F1 (critical, security)** — views carrying the tenant column are **not tenant-scoped**: `BuildTenancyContext` iterates `schema.Tables` only (`context_tenancy.go:57`) and `ViewContext` has no tenancy field, so `TenantedEntities` can never include a view. **F2 (high, correctness)** — Enum / JSON / JSONB / Slice filter fields are **advertised in the schema and silently dropped by the translator**: the schema emits via `funcComparatorFor` (switches on the GraphQL type, `default: "StringComparator"`) while the translator emits via `comparatorTranslatorVariant` (parses the model's Go comparator type and `continue`s on those four families). The server returns the unfiltered set and the client believes it filtered. **F3** — views get no GraphQL surface at all; this is **unimplemented spec**, not a new feature (PRD §26.4 already declares `View → Object type (read-only — only Query fields generated)` and `docs/design/archive/GRAPHQL.md` §952 records it as *Resolved*). **F4** — a root list cannot be filtered by a related entity; relationship fields take no args and `<T>Filter` has no relationship member.

**Five additional findings from the verification pass.** **F5** — **sort drifts too**: the schema emits enum values through `screamingSnakeCase` (`col_` prefix on digit-leading names per §8.5) while the sort translator switches on a bare `strings.ToUpper(toSnakeCase(...))`, so a `2010_revenue` column advertises `COL_2010_REVENUE`, matches nothing, and the sort is silently dropped — same failure class as F2, different emitter pair. **F6** — the five shipped comparator inputs are themselves **incomplete projections**: `String` omits `gt/gte/lt/lte/nlike`, `Numeric` omits `nin/nbetween`, `Time` omits `in/nin/nbetween`, `ID` omits `gt/gte/lt/lte`, and **none** expose `Null`, so `IS NULL` is unreachable from GraphQL on every nullable column. **F7** — **decimals cannot be range-filtered at all**: they route to `comparator.String`, so the schema says `StringComparator`, which has no `gt` (and PRD §26.5.3's own example cites a `translateNumericComparatorDecimal` that does not exist). **F8** — `sql.PrefixConditions` would **corrupt any `EXISTS` clause** (`prefixCondition` blindly prepends `alias + "."`), a hard prerequisite for F4. **F9** (scope reducer) — views have **no cache read path**, so making them tenanted needs no §29.5 key-grammar work, no `AffectedTenants` carrier, and no fingerprint change; F1 is much smaller than the table equivalent.

**Root cause behind F2 and F5 (design §1.3).** The filter and sort surfaces are each emitted by **two independent functions deriving the same fact from different inputs**. Nothing forces agreement, and when they disagree the schema wins at parse time and the translator wins at execution time — the field is accepted and ignored. Collapsing this to one derivation (25.1) and making a regression unlandable (25.2) is the durable half of the phase, and the reason F5 is in scope alongside F2.

**Ticket structure.** **25.0** — PRD sync, deltas B1–B7, no code; **blocks everything** (project rule 1: unlike Phase 24, these deltas are NOT yet blessed). **Ticket A — comparator & sort projection** (25.1 one field map / D1 — closes F5 and must regenerate byte-identically otherwise; 25.2 completeness lint / D2; 25.3 complete the five families + `DecimalComparator` / F6+F7; 25.4 enum comparators / D3; 25.5 JSON/JSONB/Slice comparators / D3). **Ticket B — view tenancy** (25.6 detection + attachment / D5+D6; 25.7 read-path emission — **the security fix**). **Ticket C — view GraphQL surface** (25.8 views into `APIContext` / D8; 25.9 emission + gqlgen binding — retires the consumer's hand-written `models:` entry). **Ticket D — relationship filtering** (25.10 `sql` prefix-exempt condition / F8; 25.11 model-side `EXISTS` compilation / D10; 25.12 GraphQL projection — closes F4).

**Governing decisions (D1–D10, design §2).** D1 one field map, two emitters; D2 codegen completeness lint; D3 monomorphize the generic families per concrete type (GraphQL has no generics — keeps schema-level validation instead of runtime parse errors); D4 complete the five shipped families in the same pass (pre-release, one golden churn not two); D5 a view carrying the tenant column is tenanted exactly like a table, read path only, fail-closed, auto-detected with a per-view opt-out; D6 nullable tenant column on a view → **warn + scope**, not the hard error tables get (legitimate `LEFT JOIN`/aggregate artifact, and scoping is fail-closed either way); D7 matview `Refresh`/`RefreshConcurrently` stay unscoped and Go-client-only; D8 views become first-class `APIContext.Tables` entries with `IsView: true` rather than a parallel context type; D9 the view GraphQL surface ships **strictly after** view tenancy (shipping it first would ship the vulnerability, on by default); D10 relationship filtering lands as junction/FK-backed filter fields compiling to `EXISTS`, with filter *arguments* on relationship fields **deferred** (nested field args are unreachable through gqlgen's public API from inside the field-options walker, and the per-relationship-resolver alternative forfeits the §25.1 one-query guarantee).

**Dependency order.** 25.0 before all. Ticket A (25.1 → {25.2, 25.3, 25.4, 25.5}) and Ticket B (25.6 → 25.7) run in **parallel**. Ticket C (25.8 → 25.9) needs **both** 25.7 (D9) and 25.1. Ticket D (25.10 → 25.11 → 25.12) is independent except 25.12 wanting 25.1's field map. **Shortest path to closing the cross-tenant read hole: 25.0(B1) → 25.6 → 25.7** — Ticket B is valuable standalone and should not wait on Ticket A.

**Consumer-visible behavior changes to flag at closure:** a previously-unscoped tenanted view read **starts filtering** (the fix), and views **start appearing** in the generated GraphQL schema (opt out with `views.<n>.api.enabled: false`).

**25.0 (PRD sync — the blessing gate) complete 2026-08-25.** Deltas B1–B7 landed in `docs/PRD.md` (+205/−15, docs-only — no code, template, config, or golden change; the path-based E2E and security-review triggers were evaluated and neither fired). Phase 25's spec is now normative, so **25.1–25.12 are unblocked**. What became spec: **B1** §29 extended to views — §29.2.3 detection now reads "table **or view**" across all five rules, §29.2.4 splits the nullable-tenant rule (table = hard error, view = warning + still scoped) and makes the uniform-tenant-type check explicitly cover tenanted views (one `TenantResolver[T]` per package), and a **new §29.2.5 View tenancy** carries the scoped-surface table (all five reads yes, `Refresh` no), the "no mutation half" list, the D6 rationale, `CallOptions` parity, D7 (matview refresh never scoped), and the F9 no-cache-coupling note. **B2/B3** a new §26.4 "Comparator input projection" subsection — Rule 1 monomorphization (with the rejected runtime-parse alternative recorded), Rule 2 `isNull` iff nullable, the complete ten-family operator matrix closing F6, `DecimalComparator` closing F7, PostgreSQL-only gating for JSONB/Slice, one-input-per-(family, type-param) sharing, and "`comparator.Custom` is never projected". **B4** §26.5.3 — the phantom `translateNumericComparatorDecimal` corrected and called out, plus the **D1 "one field map, two emitters" invariant** and the **D2 bidirectional completeness lint** stated as requirements rather than implementation notes. **B5** §16.4 + a new §26.4 "Views on the GraphQL surface" closing the emitted read set. **B6** §11.1 "Relationship filters" — the `EXISTS` shape with the target's soft-delete *and* tenant predicates inside the subquery, and the three invariants. **B7** §4.9 `views.<n>.tenancy` / `.api` + six new §4.13 validation rules. Plus two §26.12 Known Limitations (relationship-field *arguments* deferred with the gqlgen-reachability reason; aggregate relationship predicates deferred).

**25.0 auto-review round (2026-08-25).** The reviewer raised 7 items against the delta; **5 were real defects and are fixed in place**, 1 was a reviewer error corrected the other way, and 2 remain open as a Ticket-D design decision. Worth recording because the worst one was self-inflicted: **Rule 2 (`isNull` iff nullable) contradicted the Sharing rule** — GraphQL input types are global, so "one `TimeComparator` project-wide" and "`isNull` only on the nullable column's comparator" cannot both hold, and the example proved it by giving non-null `createdAt` and nullable `deletedAt` the same `TimeComparator`. Fixed by making nullability a **separate input type** (`Nullable<X>Comparator`), re-keying Sharing on a (family, type parameter, **nullability**) triple, and naming that input in §26.5.3's nullable-translator sentence — which also matches the `translateNullableTimeComparator` name that already ships. Left standing, 25.3/25.4/25.5 would each have invented the naming, violating 25.0's own "no sub-item derives its own spec". Also fixed: **§11.2's stale `decimal.Decimal → Number[T]` row** (F7 was only half-corrected — the new §26.4 text contradicted it); the **view-cache claim**, which asserted flatly that views have no cache read-through path and so tenanted views need no tenant key segment — true of today's code, contradicting §27.11 ("views can be cached like tables"), and a cross-tenant cache-poisoning trap as *spec*, now scoped to the current surface and paired with §29.5's unconditional rule; a **dangling `PrefixConditions` referent** (Appendix A never documents it — its only PRD occurrence was the new reference); and **§26.5.1**, still headed "Per-table surface" with no view coverage despite 25.0's own acceptance criteria citing it. **Reviewer error, corrected the opposite way:** the review called §16.4's matview row ("Get if unique index **or** `@pk`") stale and the implementation `@pk`-only — it is not; the introspector folds a qualifying unique index into `col.PrimaryKey` (`parser/introspect/introspect_integration_test.go:1025-1043`), so §16.4 was right and the *new* wording was too narrow. Example coherence finished too (`enum ProductStatus` declared, `status` added to `CreateProductInput`).

**25.1 (one field map — the D1 collapse) complete 2026-08-25.** The structural cause behind F2 and F5 is gone: a column's filter projection and its sort-enum spelling are each decided **once**, and both emitters read the result. New `APIFilterProjection` (`InputTypeName` / `TranslatorFunc` / `Translator` / `Operators`) plus `SortEnumValue` on `APIFieldContext`; `resolveAPIFilterProjection` derives the projection from **`resolveComparatorType`** — the same function that produces the column's `*comparator.X[Y]` field on the model filter — so the input type the schema advertises is chosen by the very expression the translator has to compile against. `schema.graphqls.tmpl` reads `$f.Filter.InputTypeName` / `$f.SortEnumValue`; `buildAPIFilterFields` and `buildAPISortFields` read the same struct instead of re-deriving; `funcComparatorFor` and its `comparatorFor` funcmap registration are deleted. **F5 is closed** — a digit-leading column now emits `COL_2010_REVENUE` in the schema enum *and* matches `case "COL_2010_REVENUE"` in the generated switch (the test was verified failing-first against the old spelling). `ComparatorFamilies` became `[]APIComparatorFamily` and `shared.graphqls.tmpl`'s five hard-coded `input …Comparator` blocks now render from it, so 25.4/25.5's monomorphized families need no template edit. **Byte-identity gate met:** `make check` and `make check-examples` both clean with **zero** change under `cmd/sqlgen/testdata/` — `TestE2EGoldenFiles` passes against the committed goldens with no regeneration, which is the proof the refactor moved structure and not behavior.

**Two things 25.1 deliberately did not do, both consequences of the byte-identity mandate.** (1) Enum / JSON / JSONB / Slice columns still advertise `StringComparator` with no translator behind them — the surviving half of F2. Dropping the fallback would have removed four filter fields from the `graphql` example's goldens, and the design (§6) states three times that 25.1 must be byte-identical. What changed is that the fallback is now a single documented decision inside `resolveAPIFilterProjection` rather than an accident spread across two functions; 25.3/25.4/25.5 replace it with real inputs. The direction that must hold absolutely — a populated `TranslatorFunc` always has a populated `InputTypeName` — is asserted on every case. (2) **Sequencing correction for Ticket A:** because that fallback survives, `ValidateAPIFilterCompleteness` as 25.2 specifies it would hard-error on the `graphql` example (`document.entityType`, `profile.settings`, `user.metadata`, `asset.declaredCategories`). 25.2's own acceptance criterion "all 13 examples pass both lints with no changes to their generated output" therefore requires **25.4 + 25.5 to land first**. The tracker's stated order (25.1 → {25.2, 25.3, 25.4, 25.5} in any order) should be read as 25.1 → 25.3 → 25.4 → 25.5 → **25.2**.

**25.1 auto-review (2026-08-25).** 6/7 PASS, 1 PARTIAL, 0 blocking. The reviewer independently verified the two claims the refactor rests on — `pkColNames` and `col.PrimaryKey` cannot disagree (both derive from the same override set / the same flag in `context_table.go`), and the translator side is provably unchanged because `ff.ComparatorType` is literally `resolveComparatorType(col, cfg.Input.Dialect)`, so only the schema side could move. Three findings fixed in place: `NameWidth` became a pre-computed context field rather than a method (TEMPLATES.md §6 — it would have been the repo's first method on a generator context struct); the reverse-direction test assertion now excludes PK/non-filterable columns; an inert `dialect` knob was replaced by an explicit coverage note. **One finding carried forward as tracked:** the byte-identity gate is narrower than "13 example modules" reads — only `graphql` and `graphql_top_level` enable `api:`, and both are PostgreSQL, so three shapes go unseen where the new derivation genuinely differs (string-typed FK → `IDComparator`, `sql.NullInt64`/`NullFloat64` → `NumericComparator`, non-enum PG arrays → `StringComparator`). The first two are latent *fixes* (the old pairing could not compile), the third neutral; recorded so 25.3–25.5's golden churn is not misattributed, with a non-PostgreSQL API-enabled example as the durable fix. Also noted: `manifest/builder.go:625 comparatorForColumn` is a **fourth** comparator derivation that disagrees on string FKs, decimals and enums — manifest-only so no accept-and-ignore risk, but a cheap catch for 25.2's lint.

**25.3 (complete the shipped families + `DecimalComparator`) complete 2026-08-26.** F6 and F7 are closed end-to-end. The five shipped comparator inputs now project their Go counterparts faithfully minus `Custom` (`String` +`gt gte lt lte nlike`, `Numeric` +`nin nbetween`, `Time` +`in nin nbetween`, `ID` +`gt gte lt lte`), nullability became a **separate input type** per PRD §26.4 Rule 2 (`Nullable<X>Comparator` = same operands + `isNull`), and `isNull` now reaches the model's `Null *bool` — which required the nullable translator to stop delegating to its base, since the two no longer take the same Go type. The shared operand block works for both variants because `comparator.Nullable<X>` embeds `<X>` and Go promotes the assignments. **`DecimalComparator` closes F7:** a decimal column routes off `StringComparator` onto `Decimal`-typed operands rendered to the canonical string form, so `{ price: { gt: "100.50" } }` is expressible for the first time and compares numerically — the database coerces the parameter against a NUMERIC column rather than collating it as text.

**One structural change 25.3 forced, worth recording:** comparator-family emission became **usage-derived** (a family is emitted because a column references it, not unconditionally). This is not tidying — `DecimalComparator`'s operands are typed with the `Decimal` scalar, which is itself only declared when a column uses it, so an unconditionally-emitted block would reference an undeclared scalar and gqlgen would reject the schema. The same failure mode was latent for `TimeComparator`/`Time` — `scalar Time` is declared only when a column's Go type is literally `time.Time`, so a project binding `timestamptz` to `types.DateTime` globally emitted an undeclared-scalar reference. **FIX-141 closed it** in the fix round by generalising the rule instead of the instances: `registerColumnScalars` now registers the scalar each *operand type* names, via a `comparatorOperandScalars` table, so any family whose operands carry a use-declared scalar is covered. **Not byte-neutral:** `graphql_top_level` referenced neither a boolean nor a non-PK ID column, so `input BooleanComparator` and `input IDComparator` — previously emitted unreferenced — dropped out of its shared schema. Removing dead declarations is correct, and **FIX-140 landed the matching delta** — PRD §26.4 **Rule 3** now conditions *both* forms on usage, stated as a correctness requirement (an unreferenced input can name an undeclared scalar) rather than a size optimisation. A related pairing was needed too: a nullable decimal column declares `scalar NullDecimal` for its field type but *both* comparator forms take `Decimal` operands, so the non-null member is registered alongside it.

**25.3 auto-review (2026-08-26): two blocking defects found and fixed in place**, both of which emitted non-compiling generated code for column shapes neither PostgreSQL example can reach. (1) **Missing `"time"` import when only nullable Time translators are emitted** — `comparatorTranslatorsUseTime` still gated on `!tr.Nullable`, which was safe only while the nullable body delegated to its base; 25.3 made it inline `[]time.Time`, and `FormatOnly` rendering means goimports never repairs the gap. (2) **The decimal narrowing misfired on a decimal FOREIGN KEY** — a regression, since `DeriveFKMethod("decimal.Decimal")` is `FKStringSprint`, so such a column resolves to `comparator.ID` and pre-25.3 correctly got `IDComparator`. The narrowing is now gated on the resolved family, which is what PRD §26.4 actually says (decimal is a narrowing *of the String family*). Both are pinned by failing-first tests. Also fixed: the reverse-direction test now **derives** its expectation from `ComparatorFamilies` instead of hand-listing Go fields — the rewrite immediately caught that `DecimalComparator` and `StringComparator` both allocate `comparator.String`, so locating a translator body by allocated struct was ambiguous. Dead code from the delegation era removed. **Two items left open for `/verify` to log, both since closed:** the `TimeComparator`/`scalar Time` declaration gap (pre-existing, same shape as the `NullDecimal → Decimal` pairing, and the completion record wrongly claimed it closed — retracted, then genuinely closed by **FIX-141**), and PRD §26.5.3's now-false "nullable translators delegate to the base translator" sentence (**FIX-139**).

**Verification.** `make check` and `make check-examples` both clean. The acceptance gate "verified operator-by-operator, not spot-checked" is implemented literally: `TestComparatorFamilies_projectEveryGoOperator` parses `comparator/*.go` with `go/ast` and fails if any non-`Custom` exported field on a family has no GraphQL counterpart — so a future Go operator cannot land unprojected. The reverse direction (`TestComparatorFamilies_translatorCopiesEveryOperator`) asserts every advertised operator is actually assigned into the model. E2E pins in three example modules use a fixture where the numeric and lexicographic orderings **disagree** (`"1000.00" < "100.50"` as text, `>` as a number), so a string comparison fails the assertion in both directions; `isNull: true` / `isNull: false` are pinned over the real gqlgen server, and offering `isNull` on a NOT NULL column's comparator is a schema validation error. **Two deviations flagged:** the decimal E2E covers postgres + mysql but **not sqlite** — that example has no decimal column because SQLite has no DECIMAL type, so adding one is a schema change outside this sub-item; and "25.2's lints pass" is not assertable yet (25.2 is unimplemented), so the bidirectional family-level tests stand in for it.

**25.3 fix round (2026-08-26): 7 of 8 logged FIXes resolved, plus one new blocking bug found and fixed.** Both blocking entries were spec-text corrections landed as PRD deltas — §26.5.3's nullable-translator paragraph (FIX-139) and a new **§26.4 Rule 3** making the emission rule read on the family rather than the nullable twin alone (FIX-140, spec amended to match code). FIX-141 generalised the scalar pairing to register whatever scalar each *operand type* names, which closed a real undeclared-`Time`-scalar bug for any project binding timestamps to `types.DateTime` globally. FIX-144 needed no schema change and answered its own question: SQLite's REAL affinity coerces the text parameter, so decimal ordering is correct on all three dialects. FIX-145 stays open by design — 25.4/25.5 delete the fallback it describes.

**25.3 verified + closed 2026-08-26.** The `/verify 25.3` re-verify pass (after the fix round) returned **9/9 PRD requirements PASS, 0 FAIL**, with 5/6 required tests present — the sixth, the cross-dialect sweep, is PARTIAL by a documented and independently re-checked invariance argument (`nlike` / `nbetween` / `isNull` take no `sql.Dialect` anywhere on their path, so only `nin` needed the four-dialect sweep it got). Every E2E pin was confirmed to actually execute rather than skip, including the SQLite decimal leg. `make check` clean (33 modules, `-race`), `make check-examples` clean (13 modules, 0 lint issues), and `TestE2EGoldenFiles` run **explicitly** — it skips under `-short`, so `make check` does not cover it, and that gap is worth remembering at closure. The independent reviewer agreed on all nine and found two items the parent missed, logged as **FIX-148 / FIX-149 / FIX-150** and all three resolved the same day. **The one with teeth was FIX-148:** the float sibling of FIX-147, in the same helper — `isSizedIntGoType` gated the input-translator cast on integer widths only, so a `real` / `float4` / MySQL `float` column (model `float32`, gqlgen `Float`→`float64`) emitted non-compiling create/update translators on **all three** arms, wider than FIX-147's nullable-only shape. Fixed at the predicate (`isNarrowedNumericGoType`, one added case); no template or funcmap logic needed, since `PointerCast` keys on `CastTo`. Zero golden movement. FIX-150 also repaired `fixes.md`'s own `## Open` / `## Resolved` boundary, which Phase 25's inserts had consumed, leaving forty-plus resolved entries readable as Open.

**25.4 (enum comparators, monomorphized per enum) complete 2026-09-01.** The enum half of F2 is closed end to end. An enum column's filter field is now typed `<EnumGraphQLName>Comparator` (or `Nullable<…>` per Rule 2) with `eq neq in nin` operands typed as the **bound GraphQL enum**, and `translate<…>Comparator` returning `*comparator.Enum[models.<T>]` behind it. `{entityType: {eq: SPV}}` narrows; `{eq: BANANA}` and `{eq: "asset.primary"}` are both rejected by the GraphQL parser before any resolver runs — the schema-level-validation benefit that motivated D3 over runtime parsing. The routing rides 25.1's single derivation (an `Enum` arm on `comparatorTranslatorVariant`, built like `makeOpaqueTranslator`), and the per-enum family rides `collectComparatorFamilies`'s union hook with **no shared-schema template edit** — which also finished wiring that hook: 25.1's own reviewer note flagged that a non-shipped family would arrive with `Operators: nil` and render the empty-input shape gqlgen rejects, and `comparatorOperatorsFor` now supplies the set.

**Two things 25.4 had to thread that no earlier family needed.** (1) `*comparator.Enum[OrderStatus]` names neither the GraphQL enum nor the models-qualified Go type, so `mapColumnToGraphQL` resolves an `enumComparatorBinding` from `typeBindings` and threads it into the projection — and `typeBindings` gained `modelsPkg` (`cfg.Output.Package`), because an enum's type parameter is the **consumer's** Go type where every other family's is a builtin or stdlib type. That makes `comparator_translate_gen.go` import the consumer models package for the first time, gated on an enum translator actually being present since an unused import is as fatal as a missing one under `FormatOnly`. (2) The guard is load-bearing, not defensive: `resolveComparatorType` routes *every* unqualified PascalCase Go type through `comparator.Enum[T]` (`isEnumLikeType`), so a consumer-scalar column spelled that way lands on the same arm and must stay on the fallback; a MySQL SET reaches the enum lookup too but resolves to `comparator.String` and never enters the arm. Both boundaries, plus the enum-array boundary with 25.5, have their own tests.

**FIX-173 — a live consumer-facing runtime bug the E2E surfaced, pre-dating Phase 25 by fourteen phases.** `comparator.Enum[T].In` / `.Nin` failed **outright** on pgx: the array-parameter path hands the driver `[]models.UserRole` and pgx has no encode plan for a slice of a named enum type against the column's enum-array OID. Reproduced from the **Go client with no GraphQL involved** — 25.4 only made it reachable over HTTP. `Eq` was unaffected, since a scalar named string goes through the driver's string-kind conversion, which is exactly why every existing enum filter test passed while the set operators were unreachable. Fixed by having `Enum.Parse` take the expanded `IN ($, $, …)` form on **every** dialect: it asks the driver for nothing beyond what `Eq` already proved, and the placeholder cost is bounded by the enum's declared members rather than by the caller — the opposite of `ID.In`, where the array parameter earns its keep. **The first attempt was worse and the user caught it:** rendering the values as `[]string` so `= ANY($1)` encodes does work (measured), but needed `reflect.TypeFor[T]().Kind()` to separate a string-kinded enum from an integer one, which would have been the **first use of `reflect` in a runtime core package**. Pinned at three levels, all verified failing-first: the comparator unit test across four dialects, the `postgres` example's Go-client `TestFilter_EnumSetOperators_Postgres`, and the GraphQL `in`/`nin` E2E cases.

**The FIX-147/148/151/152 blind spot narrowed again.** `assets.primary_category` (nullable `document_entity_type_enum`) was added to the `graphql` example because **every** enum column in the tree was NOT NULL — so `NullableDocumentEntityTypeEnumComparator` and its translator would have been emitted into no golden and compiled by nothing, which is precisely the gap that hid FIX-147 and FIX-148 for two phases. It also makes `assets` a second table referencing the same enum comparator, so the one-input-per-enum sharing rule is observed rather than asserted. The `mysql` example's three enum columns give the non-PostgreSQL leg for free.

**25.4 auto-review (2026-09-01): 9/9 verifiable PRD requirements PASS, 0 blocking.** The reviewer independently confirmed the four load-bearing claims — the enum guard on every reachable shape (the named-slice sibling resolves a binding but fails the `GoTypeName == typeParam` equality); the alias-divergence impossibility (`binds.modelsPkg` and `apiCtx.ModelsPackage` both read `cfg.Output.Package` from the same `cfg`); FIX-173's nil blast radius across the five other `parseIn` callers; and the template's operand shapes against committed gqlgen output. Golden completeness verified too — `sqlite`, `graphql_null_wrappers` and `graphql_top_level` declare no schema enums, so nothing went unregenerated. Three findings fixed in place (the new postgres test's comment still described the **rejected** `[]string` approach; `guidelines/SQL.md` §8's IN/NOT IN table had no note that `comparator.Enum` is now a permanent exception to the pgx row; a stray `sort.Strings`), and one comment corrected rather than the code — `buildAPIComparatorTranslateFile` overstated its fail-soft, which is pre-existing degeneracy on a path where the file could not compile either way. **Two FIXes logged rather than guessed at: FIX-174** (since retracted — see below) and **FIX-175** (a schema enum PascalCasing onto a shipped family name silently gets the wrong translator — the accept-and-ignore class D1 exists to eliminate, reintroduced through the name space rather than through a second derivation; the resolution is a design decision §26.4 does not cover, and 25.5 widens the same surface, so it stays open and gates 25.5).

**FIX-174 verified before starting 25.5, found wrong, and retracted — the probe found a worse defect beside it (2026-09-01).** FIX-174 claimed `comparator.Slice[T]` carried FIX-173's pgx encode defect on the array-element axis. It does not: `Slice[T]` never takes the array-parameter path, because `sliceToArrayLiteral` renders the operand as a PostgreSQL array **text literal** — `slice.go`'s doc comment says that is exactly why, "without needing custom type OID registration for enum arrays". All four operators measured clean against a real enum-array column on pgx. The entry was written from the FIX-173 analogy without reading `slice.go`, and is retracted on the record rather than deleted. **What the probe did find is FIX-176 (blocking, phase-11 origin, fixed):** the literal was built with `fmt.Sprint` joined on `","` and **no quoting**, so the function owned PostgreSQL's array-literal grammar without implementing it. Measured against a real `text[]` column, `with,comma` returned **0 rows with no error** — split into two elements server-side, so the predicate matched a different set — while `with"quote` and `with{brace}` errored with `malformed array literal` and the empty string vanished. Not SQL injection (still a bound parameter), but caller-controlled corruption of filter semantics, and the silent case is the accept-and-ignore class this phase exists to eliminate. Invisible until now because every array any test filtered on was an *enum* array, whose members are identifiers and never need quoting. Fixed with conditional quoting mirroring PostgreSQL's own `array_out`; pinned by ten unit cases plus a `text[]` E2E, both verified failing-first. **This is the justification for verifying before 25.5:** `<Elem>SliceComparator` is about to put `containsAny: [String!]` on the GraphQL surface with caller-supplied values. `guidelines/SQL.md` gained §8.1 recording the grammar.

**25.5 (JSON / JSONB / Slice comparators, monomorphized) complete 2026-09-02.** The second half of F2 is closed, and with it Ticket A's family work: `users.metadata` moved from `StringComparator` to `NullableJSONBComparator`, `profiles.settings` to `NullableJSONComparator`, `assets.declaredCategories` to `DocumentEntityTypeEnumSliceComparator`, and the five `numeric_widths` array columns onto shared `IntSliceComparator` / `FloatSliceComparator` inputs. Every one of those fields was previously advertised and ignored. **JSON and JSONB are static families, only Slice is monomorphized** — both document comparators are single Go structs, so they are ordinary `shippedComparatorFamilies` rows reached through `makeNonNumericTranslator`; Slice is the one family whose operand type varies, and it rides 25.1's `collectComparatorFamilies` union hook with no shared-schema template edit. **Both PostgreSQL-only gates are structural rather than a dialect switch**, which the task's wording would have licensed: `resolveSimpleComparator` returns `JSONB` only under postgres, and `gotype`'s `IsSlice` is set at exactly two sites, both inside the postgres array branch, with `goTypeFromOverride` never setting it — so no `overrides.types` / `type_map` / `column_map.type` path reaches a PostgreSQL-only family off postgres (independently re-verified by the auto-review). An explicit switch would have been a second spelling of a fact another function already owns, which is the shape this phase exists to remove. **The `<Elem>SliceComparator` split IS the `NumericComparator` split**: §26.4 keys an input on the GraphQL operand type, so `smallint[]` / `integer[]` / `bigint[]` share one input and split only on the translator, with the Go-width suffix appended exactly when a cast is needed. **`sliceOperandElemGoType` admits the spec built-ins only** — gqlgen's list-element pointer rule is established per type by measurement, so an array whose element binds to a custom scalar keeps the fallback rather than getting operands the body cannot copy; recorded in **PRD §26.12**. A new `filter_probes` table gives the base `JSONBComparator`, both slice nullabilities, the Boolean element binding and the no-cast Float element a compiled home, and — deliberately a `text[]` rather than another enum array — re-pins FIX-176's comma- and quote-bearing elements through the caller-supplied `containsAny: [String!]` surface. **FIX-145 closed** by the sub-item its own terms named; `fallbackComparatorInput` survives but now covers only the two shapes where no projection is *correct* (a non-schema-enum PascalCase Go type; an unmeasured array element), which is **25.2's** call. `make check` clean across all modules under `-race`; `make check-examples` clean across all 13.

**25.5 auto-review (2026-09-02): 8/8 verifiable PRD requirements PASS, 6/6 required tests present.** The reviewer confirmed the seven claims the sub-item rests on — the structural dialect gates, the collision-freedom of the slice translator names (`numericSuffixForType` is injective over the cast domain and the identity case never emits a suffix, so one FuncName can never carry two return types), enum-element detection across the named-slice and bare-array shapes, the legality of the `string(...)` document conversion for every binding the `JSON` scalar can take, and import correctness in both directions. One finding fixed in place: two doc comments still described the pre-25.5 world. **Two findings logged rather than fixed, both spec-level, and the first MEASURED against a real PostgreSQL.** **FIX-177 (blocking)** — `comparator.JSON`'s postgres arm emits `col @> $` / `col ? $`, and PostgreSQL defines neither operator for the `json` type: `settings @> $1` fails with `operator does not exist: json @> unknown (SQLSTATE 42883)` on the example's own `profiles.settings JSON`, while `settings::jsonb @> $1` matches correctly. **Not a 25.5 regression** — the column was previously accepted and silently unfiltered (the F2 class) and is now a loud error — but it is **not an implementation bug**: PRD §11.2's dialect table specifies exactly the SQL that fails, four lines after the same section explains that a postgres `json` column uses the JSON comparator *because* `json` lacks the JSONB operators. The two sentences contradict each other and the measured fix rewrites normative text, so it is flagged for a decision rather than guessed at (project rule 1). **FIX-178 (tracked)** — the SQLite half: `JSON.Parse` has no sqlite arm, so a `JSONComparator` filter there parses, dispatches into a real translator, and emits no SQL; §11.2 defers SQLite JSON while §26.4 says SQLite falls back to `JSONComparator`, so each section is self-consistent and together they specify an accept-and-ignore field that 25.2's lint cannot catch.

**Two pre-existing observations from 25.4, neither in scope and neither fixed.** (1) ~~**`or: [A, B]` on the GraphQL surface means `A AND B`.**~~ **Fixed by FIX-195 (2026-09-10).** Pre-fix, each `Or` entry was wrapped in its own `sql.Or(...)` and the wrappers were AND'd together, so two sub-filter entries produced two OR-of-one groups — a far easier trap over GraphQL, where `or: [{…}, {…}]` reads as a disjunction to every client, and it was reported as exactly that by a consumer app. The decision this called for has been taken: PRD §11.1 is amended so members OR together and a member's own fields AND together. The GraphQL surface and its translator were already correct and are unchanged. (2) **`comparator.Slice[T]` carries FIX-173's defect on the same axis** — an enum-array column's `@>` / `&&` operators still hand pgx a `[]Enum`. Unreachable today (an enum array keeps the fallback); recorded on FIX-173 so **25.5 confirms it before projecting `<Elem>SliceComparator`**. **Settled 2026-09-01 and again by 25.5's E2E:** the claim was wrong and was retracted as FIX-174 — `Slice[T]` never takes the array-parameter path, because `sliceToArrayLiteral` renders the operand as a PostgreSQL array **text** literal. The probe that retracted it found FIX-176 instead (unquoted array elements), and 25.5's `containsAny` E2E now exercises both a comma-bearing and a quote-bearing element through the live server.

**Audit of the FIX-147/148 bug class (2026-08-26) — the class was NOT closed by fixing its two known members, and a second, unrelated class was found alongside it. Both are `blocking`: `sqlgen generate` fails outright.** FIX-148 was treated as "the second bug in a known blind spot"; an exhaustive enumeration over `gotype.go`'s mapping tables showed it was the second of **seven**, and the audit surfaced a separate naming defect too. Both were confirmed **end-to-end** against the real `graphql` example with real gqlgen v0.17.90, not inferred.
- **FIX-151 (blocking)** — the coercion class has five unfixed members: `[]byte` (`bytea`; MySQL/SQLite `blob` / `binary` / `varbinary` / `tinyblob` / `mediumblob` / `longblob`), `time.Duration` (`interval`), `net.IP` (`inet`), `net.IPNet` (`cidr`), `net.HardwareAddr` (`macaddr`). The precise class is *a Go type mapping to a **spec built-in** GraphQL scalar that is not the type gqlgen binds that scalar to* — custom scalars are immune because they get a `models:` binding to the model's own type. **`bytea` is the severe one**: a completely routine column that makes generation fail. Critically, **widening the cast predicate — the FIX-147/148 fix shape — is the wrong fix for four of the five**: `time.Duration` and `net.IPNet` cannot be converted from `string` at all, and `net.IP` / `net.HardwareAddr` *would* compile but yield garbage (reinterpreting the text's raw bytes rather than parsing). Those need parse functions that can fail, in translators with no error return. The architectural fix is to register them as custom scalars the way `uuid.UUID` / `decimal.Decimal` / `types.DateTime` already are, which removes the coercion entirely.
- **FIX-152 (blocking)** — independent cause, same blind spot: the filter and input translators reference gqlgen-emitted struct fields using **sqlgen's** flect-derived field name rather than gqlgen's. Six divergent tokens found in a ten-name sweep, in **both** directions (`mac`→`MAC`/`Mac`, `pk_val`, `fk_val`, `io_count`, `os_name`, and `ascii_art` where gqlgen uppercases and sqlgen does not). `APIFilterField.GoFieldName` is *documented* as gqlgen's naming and *assigned* the model's. The project already identified this exact hazard and guarded the **sort** surface against it (PRD §26.5.3, `APISortField`'s doc) — filter and input never got the equivalent guard.

**The structural lesson, recorded for phase closure:** three consumer-facing generation failures (FIX-147, FIX-151, FIX-152) have now hidden in the same gap — the API-enabled examples (`graphql`, `graphql_top_level`) carry **zero** columns of any suspect type, while the examples that do carry `bytea` / `blob` / `interval` (`mysql`, `sqlite`, `tenancy`) have **no API enabled**. The two sets never intersect. FIX-135's proposed broader example is no longer a nice-to-have; and the durable gate is a codegen test enumerating **every** Go type in `gotype.go` against the input coercion, so the class closes by construction rather than one member per discovery.

**Open FIXes: three — FIX-183, FIX-184, FIX-185 (all `tracked`, all phase 25, all resolvable before `/close-phase 25`).** **FIX-184** (`cmd/sqlgen/manifest/builder.go` + PRD §30.4.2, from the 25.9 auto-review): every manifest entity's `files[]` hardcodes the `file_per_table` spelling and never reads `cfg.Output.Layout`, so under the default `single_file` layout it names a Go file that does not exist — `graphql` claims `workspace_note_gen.go` against a real `models_gen.go` / `views_gen.go`, and six other example trees are in the same state. It survived because `sqlite` *does* set `file_per_table`, so its entry is correct and no golden disagreed, and because `Build` already has a `layout` local — read from the unrelated `manifest.json_layout` knob. Third member of the FIX-180 / FIX-182 "the manifest names things that do not exist" family; wants their AST/golden drift guard, not a point edit. **FIX-185** (`templates/{table,view}/pagination.go.tmpl`, from the same review): a connection query that does not select a cursor-key field encodes a **zero-valued** cursor, because `Connection` passes the caller's `FieldOptions` straight to `GetMany` and nothing unions the keyset columns into the projection — so the key is never fetched and `encode<T>Cursor` serializes the struct's zero. Measured on a live container: `after:` with that cursor returns *the same row*, and `endCursor` is zero again, so a client paginating on `endCursor` loops on page 1 indefinitely. **Identical on tables** (reproduced on `users`), so it is neither a view regression nor a tenancy leak — every existing connection E2E happens to select `id`, which is exactly why it was invisible. **FIX-183 (tracked, phase 25, `parser/view.go` + PRD §16.3).** `ARRAY_AGG` resolves to Go `[]any` and an unrecognised aggregate to `any`; neither has a scan target or a GraphQL binding, so codegen refuses the column (fail-closed, PRD §26.4.1 — pinned by `TestBuildAPIContext_viewColumnWithNoGraphQLBindingIsRejected`) and a view using either needs a hand-written `@type` before it can be read over GraphQL. A capability gap rather than a correctness bug, and deliberately **unlike** the `JSON_ARRAYAGG` defect 25.8 fixed beside it, which failed *open*. The fix wants a PRD §16.3 amendment first, so it moves on its own; resolvable before `/close-phase 25`. **FIX-182 (blocking) was logged and resolved 2026-09-03**, and **closed FIX-181 with it.** FIX-181 was logged the same day while implementing FIX-180 — `Increment` is generated but appears in no manifest entity's `methods[]`, even though `features.cache.invalidates_on` already names it. Verifying its three shape decisions against `increment.go.tmpl` found the missing method was one symptom of a much larger defect, and FIX-182 superseded it: **the manifest's entire `methods[]` vocabulary was fabricated.** `FindByID` / `FindBy<Col>` / `List` / `Walk` / `Delete` / `Upsert<Target>` are generated nowhere — the real surface is `Get` / `GetMany` / `Exists` / `ExistsWhere` / `Count` / `Paginate` / `Connection` / `Stream` + `Create` / `CreateMany` / `Update` / `UpdateMany` / `UpdateWhere` / `Upsert` / `Increment` / `SoftDelete*` / `Restore*` / `HardDelete*` (PRD §9.1–§9.4a) — and the param/return types (`UserInput`, `UserUpdate`, `UserSort`, `Page`, `*UserList`) name types the package does not declare. Root cause is FIX-180's exactly: `docs/design/MANIFEST.md`'s worked example described a `c.Q.User()` / `c.M.User()` client that has never existed, PRD §30.4.2 inherited its names, and `builder.go` was implemented against the example rather than against §9. Two more live fabrications surfaced in the same block and were fixed with it — `conventions.client_entry_points` advertised that same `c.Q` / `c.M` split (real: one `client.<Entity>()` accessor per entity, §20), and `conventions.find_returns_nil_on_missing` read `true` though §10 makes `Get` a thin wrapper that *adds* `ErrNotFound`. `errors[]` was corrected in both directions (`Get` gained `ErrNotFound`; the idempotent by-PK deletes lost it per §9.5; `Update`/`Increment` now gate it on `strict_updates`; the `*Where` methods gained `ErrEmptyFilter` and `Connection` `ErrInvalidCursor`), and the tenancy guard turned out to have **four** shapes rather than two — `UpdateWhere` alone reaches `ErrMismatch` on `!inPK`. `sql_bodies` shed three false soft-delete guards and two all-column `RETURNING` clauses, gained a token set for every runtime-assembled clause (`<set>` / `<columns>` / `<values>` / `<conflict_target>` / `<excluded>` / `<column>` / `<ids>` / `<pks>`, plus `LIMIT $N` → `LIMIT <limit>` because the builder interpolates the limit as a literal), and is now **omitted entirely** on `Paginate`/`Connection`, which issue no statement of their own. **The structural guard is `cmd/sqlgen/manifest/method_drift_test.go`** — modelled on FIX-180's `sentinel_drift_test.go`, it AST-parses the checked-in generated `models_gen.go` + `views_gen.go` and asserts set equality both directions against the checked-in `manifest_gen.json`, the comparison whose absence let this ship. 189 goldens regenerated across the four manifest-emitting examples in both trees (the `graphql` example's per-entity `manifest/entities/` JSON included); `make check` and `make check-examples` clean; `schema_version` held at `0.1.0` with no JSON-schema change. PRD §30.4.2 gains the normative method-surface rule, the signature convention and the token table; `docs/design/MANIFEST.md` and `docs/design/MCP.md` are amended so the fabrication has no home left. **FIX-180 (tracked) was resolved 2026-09-03** — logged 2026-09-02 by the 25.6 auto-review follow-up (fabricated manifest error-sentinel names; see the 25.6 note below). Verification on 2026-09-03 widened it from two hardcoded sites to five: `methods[].errors[]` carried the same fabrications on every `Create` / `Update` / `Upsert` of **every** table (and under-reported tenancy errors on tenanted ones), `ErrRefreshConcurrentlyInTx` was advertised bare though §22.1 re-exports only seven sentinels and that is not one of them, and `error_sentinels[].package` was hardcoded `"models"` rather than `output.package`. The fix names only sentinels that exist, splits the constraint half into the new `conventions.constraint_error_codes` map (the four codes share one `*database.ConstraintError` discriminated by `Type`), gates the two `tenancy` rows on tenancy being configured, qualifies the sentinels at the two sites that carry no `package` field, and adds `cmd/sqlgen/manifest/sentinel_drift_test.go` — an AST-parse of the checked-in generated `errors_gen.go` asserting set equality both directions, so the list cannot drift again. PRD §30.4.1 / §30.4.2 and `docs/design/MANIFEST.md` amended; 190 goldens regenerated; `schema_version` held at `0.1.0` by user direction. **It landed before 25.7**, which therefore drops its "keep `ErrUnauthenticated` on the view block for consistency" task — the view block inherits the corrected values with no view-specific work. **FIX-177 (blocking), FIX-178 (tracked) and FIX-179 (tracked) were all resolved 2026-09-02** — FIX-178 by commit `331f006`, which this line previously failed to reflect. Both were logged by 25.5's auto-review the same day and both are **spec-level rather than implementation-level** — PRD §11.2's JSON dialect table contradicted its own "JSONB vs JSON" paragraph (a postgres `json` column is routed to the JSON comparator *because* `json` lacks the JSONB operators, and then the JSON comparator was specified to emit those operators), and §11.2's deferred-SQLite note contradicts §26.4's SQLite fallback sentence. Neither is a 25.5 regression: before 25.5 the postgres `json` column was accepted and silently unfiltered (the F2 class), and it became a loud error instead. **FIX-177's resolution took option (a) after a second measurement pass against PostgreSQL 16.15 through the real runtime path** (`comparator.JSON.Parse` → `sql.BuildCount` → pgx, not hand-written SQL). Three facts settled it: PostgreSQL gives the `json` type only `->`, `->>`, `#>` and `#>>`, so the "narrower operator set" alternative had nothing to route to; the cast is **free** where the column is already `jsonb` — the planner elides a same-type cast and a GIN index still matches (`Index Cond: (meta @> …)`), which is what lets one unconditional arm serve both column types, as it must since `Parse` never sees the SQL type; and PostgreSQL has no GIN opclass for `json` at all, so no index could have been using the broken form. `JSON.Parse`'s postgres arms now emit `col::jsonb @> $` / `col::jsonb ? $`; `Null` stays uncast; MySQL is byte-identical. §11.2 carries the amended table plus two newly-stated limits — a `json` value holding `\u0000` cannot be cast at all (unavoidable, since containment on `json` has no json-native form), and `HasKey`'s operand does not port between dialects (a bare key on PostgreSQL, a `$.`-prefixed path on MySQL), a divergence previously recorded only in a MySQL test comment. `TestJSONComparator_NarrowsOverHTTP` in the graphql example is the regression test — nine cases over the live gqlgen server against `profiles.settings json`, **verified to fail without the fix** (seven of nine raise SQLSTATE 42883; the two `isNull` cases keep passing, exactly the split the fix predicts). This was the postgres JSON family's first request-time coverage; it had none precisely because it could not run. **FIX-179 came out of FIX-177's auto-review and is the MySQL half of the same file.** `sql.PrefixConditions` blind-prepended `alias + "."` to the whole clause, which is correct only for a clause that leads with its column — and `comparator.JSON`'s MySQL arms lead with a function name, so `JSON_CONTAINS(metadata, ?)` became `u.JSON_CONTAINS(metadata, ?)`. **Reachable in the shipped mysql example with no schema change** (`users.metadata` is JSON and `users` has an O2O to `profiles`), and reproduced against a real MySQL 8.0: `Error 1370 (42000): execute command denied to user 'test'@'%' for routine 'u.JSON_CONTAINS'` — MySQL resolves it as a stored routine in schema `u`, so the message points at privileges rather than the cause. Probes ruled out the two cheap alternatives: dropping qualification is ambiguous whenever a joined table carries the same column (`ERROR 1052`), and no column-leading containment form exists in MySQL (`->>` is extraction-equality, `MEMBER OF` puts the value first).
