The fix gives `sql.Condition` a `Column` field naming the token alias-qualification rewrites; `prefixCondition` qualifies it in place when set and keeps the prepend when empty, so `sql.Raw` / `Custom` is unchanged. `ConditionBuilder` and the 21 comparator clause constructors populate it. **Zero generated-code churn** — the change stays inside `sql/` and `comparator/`. PRD §11.3 records the third field. FIX-145 was closed by 25.5, the sub-item its own terms named — JSON / JSONB / Slice columns now carry real inputs with real translators, so the advertised-and-ignored fallback is gone for all four families 25.4/25.5 covered. `fallbackComparatorInput` still exists but now covers only the two shapes with no *sound* projection (a non-schema-enum PascalCase Go type; an array element whose gqlgen list shape is unmeasured — PRD §26.12), which is **25.2's** decision to make. FIX-175 was resolved in 25.4, FIX-174 retracted as not reproducible with FIX-176 resolved in its place, and FIX-151 / FIX-152 resolved 2026-08-28/29.

**FIX-147 — a live consumer-facing bug, pre-dating Phase 25, surfaced and fixed.** Adding the nullable columns FIX-143 needed made `sqlgen generate` emit a **non-compiling** input translator for any nullable sized-int column: gqlgen binds the GraphQL `Int` scalar to Go `int` and emits `*int`, the model narrows to `*int32`, and `funcInputCoerceDeref` threaded the pointer through unconverted on the rationale that "pointers thread identically" — true only when the pointee types agree. This broke **every** consumer with a nullable `INTEGER` / `BIGINT` / `SMALLINT` column and `api.graphql` enabled, and dated from phase 16; no example had one, so nothing caught it. Fixed with a `PointerCast` flag and a deref-convert-readdress block in both translators, plus the example fixture that now compiles it. That fixture also closed FIX-143: all six nullable comparator twins are now compiled by a golden, the `NumericT` cast block under a wrapper included.

**25.7 (view tenancy read-path emission — F1, the security fix) complete 2026-09-03. Ticket B is closed.** A tenanted view's `Get` / `GetMany` / `Count` / `Paginate` / `Connection` now apply `AND <tenant> = $N`; `Refresh` / `RefreshConcurrently` do not (D7). `make check` clean across all 33 modules, `make check-examples` clean across all 13 example modules. **Two deliberate deviations from the task wording, both toward the table path's own shape:** `Paginate` and `Connection` get **no** injection of their own — both compose `Count` and `GetMany` through `internalOpts`, which forwards `SkipTenancy` / `Tenant` verbatim, so a second append would duplicate the predicate (`templates/table/pagination.go.tmpl` has no tenancy block for the same reason); and `Get` does **not** hoist the resolve to the entity-method boundary the way the table's does, because that hoist exists solely to stash `qctx.Tenant` for the cache read-through key (§29.5) and a view has no read-through path (§29.2.5, F9). The consequence worth carrying forward is that **`hook.QueryContext.Tenant` stays zero for view reads** — nothing generated consumes it, but a consumer query hook would have to resolve the tenant itself, and this is the line that moves if a view read-through path ever lands. **Three example fixtures now pair `tenancy.enabled: true` with a view**, which nothing in the tree did before: sqlite `tenancy` (`product_stats` scoped by detection + `workspace_summaries` as the explicit §29.2.5 opt-out, 8 E2E tests), `tenancy_postgres` (`article_stats` + matview `workspace_line_totals` whose `@pk` names the tenant column, 6 E2E + 2 manifest tests), and `tenancy_mysql` (`article_stats` on a CHAR(36) tenant, 3 E2E tests). Cursor pagination is walked page-by-page on two dialects to prove the tenant bind does not disturb keyset placeholder numbering, and the matview test proves an unscoped `Refresh` issued by tenant A recomputes tenant B's row. **Manifest (B9) landed with it:** `buildViewTenancyFeature` re-derives from `cfg` (never from `ViewContext.Tenancy`, which is nil under `manifest`'s own isolated `Build`) and pins `mode: "auto-filter"` as a constant, so an `@pk` tenant column cannot reach the mutation-only `"verify-match"`; `mismatch_error` gained `,omitempty` on **both** hand-duplicated `TenancyFeature` declarations, and the new reflect-based drift guard (`cmd/sqlgen/manifest/feature_drift_test.go`, verified failing before being reverted) covers `Features` / `SoftDeleteFeature` / `CacheFeature` / `EventsFeature` / `TenancyFeature` by field name, shape and json tag including options. **No schema edit and no version bump** — confirmed against the real-output check rather than by reading, with `tenancy_postgres` added to `TestManifestValidate_E2EExamples` as a `perEntity` example; `schema_version` holds at `0.1.0`. MCP needed no change and got none (`featuresSummary` already tests `Features.Tenancy != nil`), verified by a new test. **Non-tenanted output is byte-identical** — no `views_gen.go` moved in the `sqlite`, `postgres`, `postgres_stdlib`, `mysql` or `cache` examples. **Auto-review returned 7/8 PRD PASS, 1 PARTIAL, 12/12 required tests, and both deviations independently re-derived rather than accepted** — including the point that PRD **§29.9 explicitly says sqlgen "does not re-expose [the tenant] on the `QueryContext`"**, so the zero-`Tenant` consequence of the no-hoist decision is spec-conformant rather than merely harmless. **Its two findings were fixed inline, not FIX-logged.** The one with teeth was the same under-report shape as B9 one entity kind over: `buildViewMethods` hardcoded `errors[]`, so a tenanted view's five reads advertised no `tenancy.ErrMissing` even though 25.7 is precisely what made their bodies reach it (§30.4.2 — "`errors[]` lists the sentinels the generated body can actually reach"). Fixed with a `tenancyReachForView` mirroring `tenancyReachForTable`, whose `inPK` / `pkVerify` stay false by construction so a view can never advertise `ErrMismatch`; `Refresh` / `RefreshConcurrently` take none, since they resolve no tenant. The second was a doc comment naming a test that does not exist — the FIX-180/182 hygiene rule applied to this diff's own comments. **Three reviewer observations were deliberately left alone:** the inherited `CachedTenant` `apply=false` semantic (identical on the table path, not new, no poisoning vector), `docs/design/MANIFEST.md` §5.4's stale view shape and fabricated `List` method (pre-existing sibling-doc drift — `/close-phase` work), and `sql_bodies` omitting the tenant predicate for views and tables alike (pre-existing convention). **Consumer-visible behavior change to flag at closure:** a previously-unscoped tenanted view read now filters.

**25.8 (views into `APIContext` — context, config, gating; D8/D9) complete 2026-09-04. Ticket C is open and its emission half came with it.** Every API-enabled view is now an `APITableContext` in `APIContext.Tables` carrying `IsView: true`, a read-only operation set, and both `Has*Input` flags false — not a parallel context type (D8). D9 is satisfied and recorded: 25.7 closed the cross-tenant view read hole on 2026-09-03, one day before any view reached HTTP. `make check` clean across all 33 modules; `make check-examples` clean across all 13 example modules. **The tracker framed this as context-only with 25.9 turning emission on; that held only if no example paired `api:` with views — and `mysql` and `sqlite` do, through `input.views:` rather than a `views:` config block, which a grep over the configs misses.** The moment a view entered the slice, every downstream artifact that iterates it emitted: the object type, the three envelopes, `<V>Filter`, `<V>Sort` + `<V>SortField`, the columns-only field-selection walker, both translators, the envelope aliases, the resolver seeds and the gqlgen `models:` bindings — **with no per-view template arm anywhere**, which is exactly what D8 predicts. Gating views back out of those two examples to preserve the plan would have been the suppression pattern the project rejects, so the emission landed here and **25.9 shrinks to the proof layer** (the tenanted view in the `graphql` example, the F1+F3 two-tenant E2E over the real gqlgen server, the §25.1 query-count pin, the enum/JSONB filter E2E, the postgres matview check). `mysql`'s `category_stats` (no `@pk`, no `id` for `cursor_keys`) emits `categoryStatList` only; `product_summary` (`@pk: id`) emits all three Query fields; neither emits a Mutation block or a Create/Update input. **Two template deltas were genuinely required**, both because a view differs from a table rather than as accommodations: the unified client names a view's accessor **singularly** (`Client.ProductSummary()`) where a table's is plural, so the resolver template's baked-in plural spelling called a method that does not exist — now `ClientAccessor`, filled by one `entityAccessorName` the client template also reads; and the schema template's type-description fallback had the noun "table" hard-coded, so every view would have been documented as one. **Byte identity was measured, not asserted:** zero removed lines across all 14 changed shared goldens, with `generated_gen.go`'s removals reducing to one `//go:embed` line per file (gqlgen's own alphabetical re-sort); no table `.graphqls` changed, and the three view-free GraphQL example trees are untouched. **A `parser/` defect this emission surfaced, found by the user and fixed here:** `category_stats.product_names_json` shipped a **panicking gqlgen field resolver** in the golden tree. `inferAggregateType` typed every JSON aggregate as `json.RawMessage` while the table path resolves `json`/`jsonb` to `types.JSON`; both are registered under the one GraphQL scalar `JSON`, and `registerScalarUse` is first-registration-wins keyed on the name alone, so the tables' binding won and the view's Go type was silently dropped — leaving a field gqlgen could not bind and answered with `panic("not implemented")`. It compiled, so both gates stayed green. **Fixed by aligning the parser to `types.JSON`** (a *named* `json.RawMessage`, already in `knownGoTypes` and `isNativelyNilable`, so nothing moves at the scan layer) per PRD §26.4's "default for JSON/JSONB columns"; the column now also picks up 25.5's real `JSONComparator` instead of the `StringComparator` fallback. **New guard `TestE2EGoldenResolvers_NoUnboundFieldStubs`** scans every committed `expected/**/*.resolvers.go` for panic bodies and gqlgen's stale-resolver banner — nothing else in the suite reads resolver bodies, which is exactly how this shipped; verified failing-first. **The two neighbouring aggregate fallbacks were investigated and deliberately left unchanged:** `ARRAY_AGG` → `[]any` and unknown-aggregate → `any` look like the same class but are its opposite — neither has a GraphQL binding, so codegen **refuses to generate**, naming the view, column, Go type and two remedies (§26.4.1). Fail-closed where the JSON case failed open; now pinned by `TestBuildAPIContext_viewColumnWithNoGraphQLBindingIsRejected` so a future convenience fallback cannot quietly recreate the JSON failure mode. **Auto-review returned 8/10 PRD PASS, 1 PARTIAL, 1 FAIL, 7/7 required tests, 4 findings — all fixed inline, each with a regression test verified failing-first.** It independently confirmed the byte-identity measurement, that `entityAccessorName` is the only accessor spelling in the template tree, that the seven claimed GraphQL names equal the seven emitted, and — the security question — that **no tenant escape exists**: `attachTenancyToViews` sets only fields the API path never reads, so the wrapper's skipped view-attach yields an API context identical to `Generate`'s. Its FAIL was the JSON panic above; its two other substantive finds were **dead code in this sub-item's own guard** (`ValidateAPIViewReadOnly`'s catch-all called `funcHasAnyMutation`, which the new `IsView` short-circuit answers `false` for every view — it was asserting the guard against itself; now reads the extracted `mutationSurface`) and **an unreachable validation rule** (`validateViewAPIOperations` hung off `validateAPIOperations`, which returns early at `cfg.API == nil`, so a mutation key in a view mask with no top-level `api:` block validated clean — now registered independently). **Open, tracked as FIX-183:** `ARRAY_AGG` element-type inference (`[]string` via `resolveFromSchema`, the way SUM/MIN/MAX already resolve) would give both a real scan target and a real GraphQL binding, but it amends PRD §16.3's documented fallback, needs dialect-aware array handling, and still needs `[]any` for aggregates over expressions — a design item, not an inline fix; logged as **FIX-183** (tracked) rather than carried in prose. **Consumer-visible behavior change to flag at closure:** views now appear in the generated GraphQL schema (opt out with `views.<n>.api.enabled: false`), and a view's JSON-aggregate column changes Go type from `json.RawMessage` to `types.JSON`.

**Open Ticket-D decision (blocks 25.10/25.11 only).** The review caught that 25.11's original invariant — "qualify the parent-side `EXISTS` correlation at generation time" — is **wrong**, and that 25.10 has no normative PRD text at all. `BuildSelect` emits `FROM "tasks"` unaliased while `BuildSelectJoin` emits `FROM "tasks" t`, so the correlation must render `"tasks"."id"` on one path and `t."id"` on the other; a single baked-in qualifier is invalid SQL on whichever path it did not target, on PostgreSQL and MySQL both. Surviving `PrefixConditions` is necessary but not sufficient. §11.1 now states the problem instead of asserting the broken fix, and a **B8** (Appendix A / §11.5) is needed before 25.10 — B1–B7 never covered the `sql/` affordance, so the delta list itself was incomplete. Three candidate shapes are recorded in `docs/tracker/phase-25.md` above §25.10 (structured `sql.Exists` condition / substitute-token / always-alias `BuildSelect`), with the structured condition recommended. **Neither Ticket A nor Ticket B depends on any of this** — 25.1 and 25.6 are safe to start now, and the shortest path to closing the cross-tenant read hole (25.6 → 25.7) is untouched.

**25.9 (view GraphQL emission + gqlgen binding; F3) complete 2026-09-04. Ticket C is closed.** The emission itself was already correct — 25.8 carried it, and this sub-item's diff proves it: **no production code changed.** Outside the `graphql` example tree the only additions are two test files and one test-only export. What landed is the proof layer the tracker asked for, on **two** new views chosen so each isolates one claim. `workspace_note_summary` is a regular **tenanted** view (`@pk: id`, an aggregate `document_count` from a `LEFT JOIN documents`, and — after adding `kind workspace_note_kind_enum` + `labels JSONB` to `workspace_notes` — an ENUM and a JSONB column). It projects `workspace_id`, so §29.2.5 scopes it by **detection alone**, the same path a consumer falls into by accident, and it carries `id`, so the inherited `cursor_keys` default resolves and **all three** read queries exist. `category_price_totals` is a **materialized** view over the shared `products` table, deliberately un-tenanted so "a matview reads like a view" is isolated from "a tenanted view is scoped"; its connection query exists only because `sqlgen.yml` names `cursor_keys`, which exercises §4.13's rule that views have **no primary-key fallback**. **The security pin was verified failing-first.** `tests/view_tenancy_test.go` boots the real gqlgen server behind the header→ctx tenant bridge and asserts isolation across by-PK, connection and list, plus the fail-closed no-header branch. With the two `sql.Where("workspace_id").Eq(resolvedTenant)` appends in the generated view client neutered, the by-PK test fails with *"tenant A read tenant B's view row by PK"* and both envelope tests fail on a `totalCount` of 3 where 2 was expected — so the assertion detects the F1 hole rather than merely passing beside it. The by-PK case is the sharpest form: the caller already holds the key, and it runs the `Get` → `GetMany` delegation where the predicate is actually appended. **§25.1 is measured on a tenanted view** — a counting `Querier` *plus* the tenant resolver, over nine rows: by-PK = 1, `<view>List` with `totalCount` = 2, connection with `totalCount` = 2. The 1 is §26.4's "a view read is exactly one query" (no relationships, nothing fans out); the 2s are `Count` + `GetMany`, the same shape a table's envelopes have. Wiring tenancy into the counting handler is the point — the predicate rides a statement that would have run anyway, so it must cost **zero** extra round-trips, and a shape that resolved the tenant with its own `SELECT` would pass every isolation assertion and fail here. **The matview claim is stated as an equality, not a checklist:** `TestAPIViewSchema_materializedIsIdentical` renders the same view with `Materialized` false then true and asserts the two schemas are **byte-identical**, then that neither mentions `refresh` — which holds because `Materialized` stops at `ViewContext` and never reaches `APITableContext`. The E2E half drives all three read queries against the live matview and includes a *stays stale until refreshed* subtest: a product created after the last refresh does not move `productCount`, and the only remedy is the Go-client-only `Refresh` — the observable consequence of §16.5 the GraphQL surface deliberately offers no way to fix. **Two assertions run through `__schema` introspection rather than against golden text** (the closed read set per view; no Mutation field addressing a view and no `refresh` field on any type or input), because a golden says what sqlgen wrote while introspection says what a consumer can *call* — and both are claims about the published API. **The FIX-175 claim set is now checked against real emission in both directions** (`TestAPIViewSchema_emissionMatchesTheClaimSet`): every type the view schema declares is claimed by that view in `collectGraphQLTypeNames`, and every name claimed for it is declared. The two lists are the same seven names written in two files derived from different inputs; nothing but this test forces them to agree. `buildMergeInput`'s four view bindings get their own unit test — the risk there is not that the loop is wrong today but that a future change gates it on something table-shaped (`HasCreateInput`, a non-empty `Relationships`, a mutation operation) and silently drops views back out. **One fixture consequence worth recording:** this repo's create input requires every NOT NULL column regardless of `DEFAULT`, so adding `kind` and `labels` to `workspace_notes` made the two existing `createWorkspaceNote` call sites in `tests/tenant_update_input_test.go` fail schema validation; they now pass both columns. `make check` clean across all 33 modules, `make check-examples` clean across all 13 example modules, `TestE2EGoldenFiles` + `TestE2EGoldenResolvers_NoUnboundFieldStubs` clean.

**25.10 (`sql` prefix-exempt correlated `EXISTS`; F8/D11) complete 2026-09-04. Ticket D is open.** `sql/` gained `Exists(correlationColumn string, subquery Subquery) Condition` — a constructor returning a `Condition` whose `Value` is an unexported `existsCondition` and whose `Clause` is **empty**, exactly the treatment `And` / `Or` already get. That empty clause is the whole D11 exemption: `prefixCondition` returns early on `Clause == ""` before either qualification path, and the `And`/`Or` recursion hits the same return, so alias prefixing passes the condition through **by construction** rather than through a flag a caller can forget. The parent-side reference travels as the exported `sql.CorrelationToken`; `expandExists` replaces it with `<qualifier>.<QuoteIdentifier(col)>` after renumbering (that order means a qualifier containing a `$` can never be read as a placeholder), and unlike a bare `Subquery` value — spliced in verbatim — an `Exists` subquery's `$` tokens are renumbered into the enclosing statement's positional sequence, which is what PRD §11.5 rule 3 requires and what §11.1's `$1 … $2 $3` example shows. The qualifier is threaded internally (`appendWhere` → `buildWhere` → `expandCondition` → `expandComposition`) and resolved by each builder from what its own `FROM` clause actually says: `SelectOptions.Alias` in `BuildSelectJoin`, `FormatTable(t)` everywhere else. **Extending it past the two read paths §11.5 names was deliberate** — a relationship filter reaches `BuildCount`, `BuildExists`, `BuildUpdate`, `BuildIncrement`, `BuildSoftDelete`, and `BuildHardDelete` through the same `ToConditions`, so it had to render correctly on every filter-accepting builder. No public builder signature changed, `sql/` still imports only `fmt` / `sort` / `strings`, and **nothing emits the condition yet, so there was no golden churn** — 25.11 is where it gets used. `make check` clean across all 33 modules under `-race`; `make check-examples` clean across all 13 example modules; every pre-existing `sql` test passed untouched, which is the byte-identical-output criterion. The D11 proof is written as an equality rather than two assertions — **one** `Exists` value is passed to both `BuildSelect` and `PrefixConditions` + `BuildSelectJoin` and asserted to render `"public"."users"."id"` on the first and `t."id"` on the second, because a test that built the value twice would still pass with the qualifier baked in at construction, which is the exact failure D11 exists to prevent. Verified failing-first: collapsing the join qualifier to `FormatTable(t)` fails the new tests in 17 places. **Auto-review: 11/11 PRD + acceptance criteria PASS, 5/5 required tests, 0 blocking findings, 5 nits.** It confirmed the `CorrelationToken` mechanism is not the shape D11 rejected (the token's only render path always substitutes, where the rejected leaf-clause form could reach the database unsubstituted) and that the signature changes are fully contained to `sql/builder.go`. One nit fixed inline (`d.FormatTable(t)` evaluated twice in six builders, now hoisted). **Three carry-forwards, none blocking, none FIX-logged:** (1) **MySQL error 1093** — the mutation builders now accept an `Exists`, and MySQL rejects `UPDATE t … WHERE EXISTS (SELECT … FROM t …)`; it bites only when a relationship target *is* the mutated table, and belongs in **25.11's** emission constraints; (2) passing the same `Subquery` as a plain `Subquery` value rather than through `Exists` splices the token verbatim — undetectable at runtime, guarded by 25.11's goldens; (3) `replaceDollars` returns all args even when it runs out of `$` tokens, pre-existing behavior noted only because `Exists` is its first generator-composed caller.

**25.0 placement/hygiene notes.** The two new §26.4 blocks are **h5 subsections**, not renumbered `#### 26.4.x` — following the existing `##### PK columns in the create input` precedent, because renumbering would have invalidated every cross-reference to §26.4.1 Scalar Marshaling. All 25 anchors introduced by the new text were verified against a GitHub-accurate slugger. Four **pre-existing** dangling intra-PRD links surfaced incidentally and were deliberately left alone as outside B1–B7: `#2654-increment--decrement-as-input-operators`, `#322-access-roles` (§32.2 is "Roles"), `#74-built-in-optional-types` (§7.4 is "Built-In Type Integrations"), `#98-generated-methods` (§9.8 is "Generated Method Patterns") — a candidate cleanup for the phase-closure sweep. **Next: three tickets are actionable in parallel.** **Ticket C is complete** — 25.8 gated on **both** 25.7 (D9) and 25.1 and landed 2026-09-04, so the view GraphQL surface shipped without the vulnerability D9 exists to prevent; and **25.9 landed 2026-09-04**, closing the ticket (see the 25.9 paragraph). Ticket A has 25.3/25.4/25.5 landed and continues at **25.2** (the completeness lint, which must come last — see the sequencing correction above). Ticket D starts at **25.10**, unblocked since D11/B8 landed 2026-08-25. **Ticket B is now complete — see the 25.7 paragraph below.** Its first half: **25.6 landed 2026-09-02** (detection + attachment — `views.<n>.tenancy`, view participation in the §29.2.4 uniform-type check, D6 nullable warn-and-scope, `ViewContext.Tenancy`, tenanted views in `ClientContext.TenantedEntities`), and **25.7 landed 2026-09-03** — the read-path predicate emission that actually closes it. 25.6's one deliberate transient is gone: the unified client had been threading `tenantResolver` into tenanted views against a field `templates/view/client.go.tmpl` did not declare, so a project pairing `tenancy.enabled: true` with a tenant-carrying view generated a package that did not compile at the 25.6 commit. Three examples now pair them and all compile. **One 25.6 auto-review finding became a PRD delta plus 25.7 tasks (B9):** `manifest.buildViewEntity` sets `Features{}` unconditionally, so a tenanted view reports `features.tenancy: null` while a tenanted table reports it fully — telling the MCP server and any agent reading the manifest that a scoped view's rows are globally visible. The table shape does not transfer (`mode: "verify-match"` is a §29.4.2/§29.7 mutation rule a `@pk`-annotated view can trip; `mismatch_error` names an error a view can never return), so **§30.1 and §30.4.2 were amended 2026-09-02** to specify the read-only-entity shape — `mode` always `"auto-filter"`, `mismatch_error` omitted — and to record that `features.cache` on a cached view stays `null` pending its own spec pass. **Implementation is assigned to 25.7**, where the tenanted-view example that pins it lands; the change is invisible in goldens until then. Confirmed to need no `schema/v1.json` edit, no manifest version bump (the `0.1.0` freeze holds), no markdown change, and no MCP change. Two pre-existing observations now have owners: the hand-duplicated `TenancyFeature` struct (builder-side vs runtime `manifest`) gets a **required** drift-guard task in 25.7, which is the first change to diverge the two intentionally; and the fabricated error-sentinel names are logged as **FIX-180** (tracked, **resolved 2026-09-03** — see the open-FIX line above) — `missing_resolver_error: "ErrUnauthenticated"` / `mismatch_error: "ErrForbidden"` plus four of the five `conventions.error_sentinels` are PascalCased GraphQL `extensions.code` values from §26.5.5 that no runtime package, template, or generated file declares, while six real sentinels go unadvertised. It spans every tenanted table, needs a PRD §30.4.1 amendment, and churns 16 manifest goldens, so it moves on its own. *(On investigation it proved wider — five sites, not two, and 190 goldens; it landed 2026-09-03, ahead of 25.7.)*

## Prior Focus (archived)

**Phase 23 (Cache × Tenancy — Nil-Tenant Invalidation) closed 2026-07-15.** All 6 sub-items complete, same-day: 23.1 structural tenant capture (`m.AffectedTenants []any` carrier, widened `RETURNING` PG/SQLite incl. pgx `SendBatch`, MySQL pre-reads incl. the new D10 batched `*Many` pre-read, `sql.BuildIncrementReturning`); 23.2 B.0 invalidation rewrite (row-derived keys, hard error removed, `invalidateTenantedFallback` defensive-only net); 23.3 event tenant publish (per-row `Metadata["tenant"]` stamp, SkipTenancy events now stamped); 23.4 event-driven invalidation fix (the live §28.11 defect — `InvalidationSignal` struct, precise per-PK tenanted receive, T10 landed failing-first); 23.5 explicit-tenant `CallOptions.Tenant *T` (D2 precedence, D11 mismatch); 23.6 example wiring + T1–T11+T7b + PRD sync + design-doc SYNCED.

**23.6 closure sweep (landed 2026-07-15):** `make check` clean across all 8 modules under `-race` (~6m23s); `make check-examples` clean across all 12 example modules (~2m10s; longest leg tenancy_mysql/tests ~7.8s) — the roster grew by two this phase (`tenancy_mysql`, `tenancy_postgres`); `make test-integration` clean (~9m07s; longest legs `cmd/sqlgen/cli` ~510s, `cmd/sqlgen` ~269s, `gen` ~41s); no flakes. **FIX triage:** 0 open at closure, 0 carried forward — **the entire phase ran with an empty FIX ledger**; every reviewer finding (2 passes per sub-item) was resolved inline before each `/done`. **Surface delta summary (across Phase 23):** tenanted cache invalidation now derives the tenant **structurally from the mutated row** on every dialect/shape — `hook.MutationContext.AffectedTenants []any` (index-aligned with `AffectedPKs`), widened capture queries, and a rewritten `invalidateAffectedTenanted` replace the old resolver-dependent path, so `SkipTenancy` mutations invalidate precisely instead of hard-erroring/skipping/degrading; events carry a per-row `Metadata["tenant"]` stamp; the cross-instance §28.11 defect is fixed via the new `cache.InvalidationSignal{Table, Schema, Tenant, PKs}` handler contract (D6 break, pre-release) with precise per-PK tenanted receive; `CallOptions` gained a concretely-typed explicit `Tenant *T` field (explicit wins over `SkipTenancy`, `tenancy.ErrMismatch` on input-column disagreement); two new compile-run example modules (`tenancy_mysql` — string tenant over CHAR(36); `tenancy_postgres` — pgx SendBatch capture) guard the non-SQLite paths and immediately caught two real codegen defects at introduction. Runtime core stayed stdlib-only; non-tenanted generated output byte-identical (T8). **Next:** next feature phase per scheduling, or the deferred manifest v1.0.0 freeze track (schema tightening + version lift, PRD §30.5).

**Phase 23 (Cache × Tenancy — Nil-Tenant Invalidation) broken down 2026-07-15.** Registered in `docs/tracker/IMPLEMENTATION_ORDER.md` and decomposed into 6 sub-items in `docs/tracker/phase-23.md` from the DESIGN FROZEN doc PRD §27.9/§28.11/§29.4.4/§29.5/§29.6 (decision record D1–D11, test plan T1–T11 + T7b). Fixes a latent stale-cache class: a mutation under `CallOptions.SkipTenancy` leaves `m.Tenant == nil`, so tenanted cache keys (`tenant`-before-`fingerprint`, §29.5) cannot be built — today by-PK invalidation hard-errors in an async post-commit callback (silent staleness), create silently skips, `*Where` degrades to a table pattern. The fix derives the tenant **structurally from the mutated row** (never the resolver, never a partial returned entity), precise on every dialect/shape; stamps the same tenant into event payloads; and fixes a **live §28.11 defect** (cross-instance event-driven invalidation is broken for every tenanted table, D5). Sub-items: **23.1** structural tenant capture (`m.AffectedTenants []any`, widened `RETURNING`/pre-`SELECT`, new MySQL `*Many` batched pre-read — D1/D9/D10; **foundation, blocks 23.2–23.4**); **23.2** B.0 cache-invalidation rewrite (row-derived key, remove hard error, defensive-only fallback + signal — G1–G3/D3); **23.3** event tenant publish / E1; **23.4** event-driven invalidation fix / E2 (`InvalidationSignal` struct — D5/D6); **23.5** Axis A explicit-tenant `CallOptions` field (`Tenant *T` — D2/D7/D11; independent, may be last/deferred); **23.6** example wiring + T1–T11 + T7b + PRD §27.9/§28.11/§29.4.4/§29.5 sync + design-doc SYNCED + `/close-phase 23`. **Dependency order:** 23.1 → 23.2/23.3/23.4 (23.4 also wants 23.3's stamp); 23.5 independent; 23.6 last. **Progress:** 23.1 (structural capture) **complete 2026-07-15** — `m.AffectedTenants` carrier, widened RETURNING (PG/SQLite incl. pgx batch `br.Query()`) + `captureAffectedTenants` pre-read / widened `collectAffected*` (MySQL), Go-side create/upsert capture, `sql.BuildIncrementReturning`; alignment pin tests (real DB) + cross-dialect render/syntax-compile tests; tenancy example goldens regenerated, non-tenanted examples byte-identical; verified clean (0 FIXes). Nil-element/nil-slice carrier semantics recorded in the 23.1 completion record for 23.2's consumer. 23.2 (B.0 invalidation rewrite) implemented 2026-07-15 — `invalidateAffectedTenanted` rebuilds keys from row-derived tenants (PK struct ∈-PK / captured carrier ∉-PK, %v round-trip so 23.4's string tenant reuses it), hard error removed, `invalidateTenantedFallback` defensive net (signal + nil return, nil breaker), create/createMany write-through structural, *Where precise for tenanted tables; T1–T7b (+ nil-element skip case) live in the tenancy example; **verified clean and complete 2026-07-15** (0 FIXes, two reviewer passes). 23.3 (event tenant publish / E1) complete 2026-07-15 — per-event row-derived `Metadata["tenant"]` stamp (PK struct ∈-PK / sole-PK direct / captured carrier ∉-PK, nil → key absent), SkipTenancy events now tenant-stamped (design-sanctioned contract change, pre-E1 pins re-encoded), T9 ×4 + render coverage; §29.6 added to 23.6's PRD-sync list (reviewer catch). 23.4 (event-driven invalidation / E2) complete 2026-07-15 — the live §28.11 defect fixed: `InvalidationSignal` struct (D6 break), adapter forwards the E1 tenant stamp, generated `handleInvalidation` routes tenanted signals to the precise per-PK row-derived path (string tenant, %v round-trip, receiver-local fingerprint) with tenant-less → safe ACK'd table-pattern fallback; T10 landed failing-first then green. 23.5 (Axis A explicit tenant) complete 2026-07-15 — concretely-typed `CallOptions.Tenant *uuid.UUID` (gated on tenancy + a tenanted table), one-seam resolution via `resolveTenant(ctx, explicit)` (explicit beats ctx cache), D2 normalized once in `resolveCallOptions`, D11 via existing verify-match, T11 a/b/c live. 23.6 (example wiring + T-suite + PRD sync + design SYNCED) **complete 2026-07-15** — T1–T11+T7b consolidated green; **two new compile-run example modules** `tenancy_mysql` (MySQL 8, string tenant over CHAR(36), batched D10 pre-read + widened collects + per-row stamps) and `tenancy_postgres` (Postgres 16 + pgx, SendBatch per-item RETURNING capture), which immediately caught two real codegen defects (23.1's MySQL `_, err :=` redeclare after the tenant pre-read on soft-delete tables, fixed in `delete.go.tmpl`; a pre-existing latent `InvalidateMany` dead-code `go vet` failure when all cached tables are tenanted, fixed in `cache.go.tmpl` with `$anyNonTenantedCached`); PRD §27.9/§28.11/§29.4.4/§29.5/§29.6 synced (incl. reviewer-caught stale positional-handler sentence); PRD §27.9/§28.11/§29.4.4/§29.5/§29.6 → **SYNCED (2026-07-15)** with five recorded adjudications; T8 byte-identical holds; two clean reviewer passes, 0 FIXes — the entire phase closed with an empty FIX ledger. **Next:** `/close-phase 23` (full sweep incl. `make test-integration`, FIX triage, bookkeeping). _(Closed — see Current Focus.)_

**19.5 (`.mcp.json` emission + config block + agent-integration docs) complete 2026-07-10.** New `cmd/sqlgen/mcp/mergeconfig.go` — the full MCP.md §6.6 merge contract over a single target file: sentinel markers (`x-sqlgen-managed`/`x-sqlgen-manifest`/`x-sqlgen-generator-version`), match-by-absolute-manifest-path in-place update, unmanaged-collision → warn + `pickFreeKey` (`sqlgen-2`…), stale sweep (managed entry whose manifest file no longer exists on disk — the file-existence rule is what lets `output.dir`-move cleanup and monorepo-sibling coexistence both hold), canonical byte-stable output (sorted keys, 2-space, HTML-escape off, `json.Decoder.UseNumber` so sibling entries' numeric forms survive re-encode), atomic write (tmp → fsync → rename), `command_template` via `text/template` `{{ manifest_path }}` + shell-style tokenization (never executed by sqlgen), delete-file-when-empty, remove-with-no-match leaves the file byte-untouched. New `cli/mcp_emit.go` — pipeline step 7d (after the manifest stage; CLI coordinates because `mcp` imports `manifest`): loops `project_configs`, per-target failure isolation via `errors.Join` → non-zero exit; removal path runs with §3.4 defaults when the manifest block is absent/disabled; manifest identity path mirrors EmitJSON placement (`<output.dir>/<markdown_dir>/<json_filename>`) and honors `SQLGEN_OUTPUT_DIR_IMPORT_OVERRIDE` (FIX-062 pattern) so E2E temp-dir compare runs keep committed example `.mcp.json` byte-stable. `config.MCPConfig` block (`emit_project_config` default-true-when-enabled, `project_configs` `[.mcp.json]`, `server_key` `sqlgen`, `command_template`; global-only — §3.4 defines no table-level field) + two validation rules (emit-requires-enabled; module-root-relative, `..`/absolute/empty rejected — enforced symmetrically across enabled/disabled).
